I couldn't find anything in the code that explains 5–45 second responses. The server-side read paths are small and nothing in them blocks. What the screenshots show looks like a stall upstream that the code has no defence against. I haven't confirmed that: I can't read the production logs, and I had no signed-in session to reproduce with.

## What I ruled out in the code

- **Auth check.** The production project signs tokens with ES256, so `getClaims()` checks the token locally instead of calling the auth server. The signing keys are cached for the whole process ([GoTrueClient.js](node_modules/.pnpm/@supabase+auth-js@2.118.0/node_modules/@supabase/auth-js/dist/main/GoTrueClient.js#L42-L69)), and the check runs once per request ([session.ts:14](src/features/auth/server/session.ts#L14)).
- **Database calls per page.** `/parent/settings` makes about 3 rounds of database calls one after another: the user's session, their family membership, then 4 lookups at once ([page.tsx:29-36](src/app/(app)/parent/settings/page.tsx#L29-L36)). The child page makes about 4–5 rounds. With Vercel and Supabase both in Tokyo, that's roughly 200–400 ms on the server, not seconds.
- **Locks.** Every SQL function the pages call while loading is a `stable` read: `list_due_for_parent`, `balance`, `list_ledger_history`, `is_parent_of`, `allowance_next_due_on`. None takes a lock or writes, so pages loading at the same time can't queue behind each other in the database.
- **Refresh loops.** No client code keeps calling `router.refresh()` or `replace`. The repeated `settings` and child-page rows match you clicking around while earlier page loads were still pending.
- **Baseline speed.** Public routes on production respond in about 150–350 ms over HTTP/2, so the network and CDN are fine.

## The clue in the screenshots

The finished times, in the order the requests started, are 45.5 s, 11.2 s, 7.1 s, 5.1 s and 5.1 s. Each started later than the one before, so they most likely all finished at the same moment. That points to one thing blocking every in-flight request, not five slow queries.

The likely causes are all outside the code:
1. A Supabase call hanging on a stale connection or a slow free-tier database.
2. A Vercel cold start, or a function instance that had stalled.

## Problems in the code that make it worse

1. **No timeout on Supabase calls.** Neither [server.ts](src/lib/supabase/server.ts) nor [proxy.ts](src/lib/supabase/proxy.ts) passes a custom `fetch` with a time limit. If one call stalls, the page waits for as long as Supabase takes, the 45 seconds here, instead of failing fast to the error page. This is the main fix I'd make.
2. **The gear link costs an extra trip.** It points to `/settings`, which loads the session and membership just to redirect to `/parent/settings` ([settings/page.tsx:11-15](src/app/(app)/settings/page.tsx#L11-L15)). That's a full extra server round trip on every click. The header could link straight to the right settings page for the user's role.
3. **Nothing measures timing.** No log or `Server-Timing` header covers repository calls, so there's no way to tell whether the time goes on Supabase or Vercel.

## To find the actual cause, someone needs to check

- **Vercel:** the project is in `haris-shakeels-projects`, which your account can't see. Someone with access should check Observability → Functions for the durations of these requests and any timeouts.
- **Supabase:** in the dashboard, check Reports → API for slow requests and CPU/memory, and whether the project is on the smallest free-tier instance.

Want me to add the 8-second timeout on Supabase calls (fix 1), the direct settings link (fix 2), and per-call timing logs (fix 3)?

Both research tracks are back. Here's what matters most.

**What prior art says**
- Money apps don't move money offline. They save what the user meant to do, and the server makes the final decision when it reconnects. This matches Jarso's ledger design.
- When an offline action is sent and refused, good apps keep it on screen as "Couldn't send", with a plain reason and the choices to edit, retry or remove. It never quietly disappears, and money is never quietly taken.
- iPhone Safari can't send things in the background. Queued items can only go when the app is open, comes back into view, or the connection returns. For a lot of 11–14-year-olds, that means "next time they open Jarso".

**What the codebase already has, and what's missing**
- **Next 16 offline mode** (`useOffline`) keeps failed page loads and actions waiting and resends them when the connection returns. It only holds them in memory, though, so a reload or a closed tab loses them. A durable on-device queue would have to be built on top.
- **Retry safety:** purchases, handovers, "not given" and mission progress already survive a resend without creating duplicates. Two actions don't:
  - **`create_gift`:** a double tap creates two gifts.
  - **Money guide question:** a resend asks Claude again and records a duplicate turn.
- **Stale sessions:**
  - **Concurrent refreshes:** a tab left idle and then reopened on a slow network can fire several token refreshes that arrive more than 10 seconds apart. Supabase treats that as token reuse and signs the user out.
  - **Removed users:** a removed user keeps a valid sign-in for up to an hour. The database still blocks them.
  - **Sign-out:** it ignores failures, so on a bad network it can fail and leave the user signed in.
- **Work that can hang:** a money guide question can run for about 2 minutes, with no overall time limit and no stop when the user leaves. Supabase calls have no timeout at all, which is your 45-second hang.
- **No browser-side leaks** today: the in-browser Supabase client isn't used anywhere.

**My guess for your earlier question:** a child's purchase that's refused after waiting stays in their diary as "Needs a look". It shows a kind reason, something like "Your Spend jar only has ₹50 left", plus three buttons: change it, ask a parent, or remove it.

The research says parent confirmations (handovers, gifts) should stay online-only: they depend on fresh balances and on what the other parent has already done. When a parent confirms one offline, should it also wait on the device and send later, or should the app say "You're offline, try again when connected"?

The reviewer found a real problem, and I've settled it myself. Next's built-in offline mode quietly resends a parent's handover minutes later, which breaks your "try again when connected" rule. So I've dropped that mode. Instead, Jarso detects when it's offline itself, and the saved actions send through their own path. I also put the Supabase sign-in setting in as a step in the deploy guide, since changing it needs someone with dashboard access.

Three questions only you can answer, most important first:

1. **Which time does a saved action carry?** A purchase saved on Monday and sent on Tuesday currently lands on Tuesday. It shows on the wrong diary day, counts toward the wrong spending limit, and could clash with Tuesday's check-in. Should it keep the time the child actually did it? That takes a database change, plus a rule capping how far back that time can be.
2. **Should a child be able to open Jarso from scratch with no internet and still add a purchase?** As designed, saving offline works only if the app was already open when the connection dropped. Opening it cold while offline shows the browser's "no internet" page. Covering cold starts means making Jarso an installable offline app, which is a big chunk of extra work.
3. **What does "ask a parent" on a refused purchase do?** It could just be advice in the message, with the item staying put. Or it could send the parent a notification they can act on, which needs a new notification type and a parent-side screen.

The design is updated with your three answers: [2026-10-30-network-resilience-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-30-network-resilience-design.md).

These are calls I made myself, so check them while you read:
- **Next's offline mode is off.** It would quietly resend parent actions later. Jarso detects being offline itself, and parent actions give up after about 15 seconds, showing your offline warning with the form still filled in.
- **Saved actions send through their own path,** not the normal form submit. That way they can tell "no internet" apart from "refused", and a new deploy doesn't break them.
- **Time limits:** about 8 seconds per database call and about 45 seconds for a whole money guide answer. A page that's taking a while switches to a "Try again" message after about 10 seconds.
- **Gift creation and money guide questions get duplicate protection,** so a double tap or resend can't create a second gift or ask Claude twice.
- **The Supabase sign-in setting becomes a step in the deploy guide,** because changing the live project needs someone with dashboard access. The code-side fix ships either way.
- **Saved items give up after 7 days or 20 failed tries.** They then show "This one waited too long. Add it again?"

Once you've read it, are you happy for me to turn this into the step-by-step build plan?

The plan is saved to [2026-10-30-network-resilience.md](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-30-network-resilience.md). Please review it. It has 13 tasks, each with its tests and commits:

1. **Timed Supabase calls:** every database call gives up after 8 s with a "Jarso is taking a while" message, and calls over 1 s are logged. A slow sign-in check no longer kicks people to the login page.
2. **Money guide:** each answer is capped at 45 s.
3. **No duplicates:** a double tap or resend can't create a second gift or ask the guide the same question twice.
4. **Device time:** saved actions keep the time the child did them, up to 7 days back.
5. **Offline banner,** and a wrapper that stops parent and account actions with your offline warning.
6. **On-device outbox:** saves the child's actions, retries them, and makes sure two open tabs never send the same one.
7. **One server endpoint** that receives saved child actions.
8. **Diary purchases:** they save offline and get the "Waiting to send" and "Needs a look" states.
9. **Missions** keep going when the connection drops.
10. **Every parent and account form** is switched to the offline warning.
11. **Slow pages** show "This is taking a while. Try again?" after 10 s.
12. **Sign-out failures are logged,** and the Supabase setting change goes into the deploy guide.
13. **Full verification,** including a manual run on throttled and offline networks.

Choices the plan makes for you to check:
- **Starting a mission is online-only.** The server has to create the mission before the child can play it, so that one tap can't be saved for later. Steps and finishing a mission that's already started do work offline.
- **Pages still refresh tokens as now.** Skipping refreshes on background page loads would lose renewed sign-ins. The longer 30-second reuse window plus the 8 s time limits cover the problem instead.
- **Two small new libraries:** `idb` (a thin wrapper around the browser's storage) and `fake-indexeddb` (for tests only).

To keep it clean and maintainable, each piece lives in one place:
- one shared timed fetch for all three Supabase clients;
- one offline module that every form uses;
- one outbox endpoint that calls the existing services, so validation and permission checks aren't duplicated;
- a single tested function that decides each saved action's next status.

There are two ways to build it:
- **Subagent-driven:** a fresh agent builds each task, and a separate reviewer checks it before the next one starts.
- **Native:** I build every task myself in this session, and one reviewer checks the whole branch at the end.

I recommend subagent-driven: tasks 5 to 10 all build on each other's interfaces, and a mistake there could double-record money.

Does the plan capture what you want, and which approach should I use?

[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_PROMPT num=1 session=f88ac5af-0f6c-4ec8-b8bf-1fe3f91a8e7c agent=ace69f169610e68b7]

Research task (web research, no code changes). Context: Jarso is a pocket-money learning web app (Next.js 16 App Router + Supabase, deployed on Vercel) for children aged 11-14 and their parents. Parents confirm allowance handovers / gifts; children log purchases into a money diary that debits a "Spend" jar, subject to parent money rules (e.g. overspend not allowed, negative balance allowed or not). Users are often on high-latency or flaky networks (school Wi-Fi, mobile data). The product owner wants: when a write action fails due to network, save it locally on the device and auto-send when connectivity returns.

Open question to research: what happens when a queued offline action is REJECTED by the server once it finally syncs (e.g. balance now too low, rule blocks it, item was already handled by the other parent, session expired), and how should that be presented — especially to a child?

Please find and summarise, with source URLs:
1. Prior art / UX patterns for offline-queued writes that later get rejected: e.g. banking/fintech apps (Monzo, Revolut, GoHenry, Greenlight, Rooster Money), Google Docs/Gmail outbox, Todoist, Linear, Notion, WhatsApp "pending" ticks, Figma. How do they show "pending", "sent", "couldn't send — here's why", and what choices they give (retry, edit, discard).
2. Guidance on which actions are SAFE to queue offline vs must be online-only (money movement, authorization-sensitive actions). Any fintech guidance that money mutations should not be optimistic/offline.
3. Technical mechanics on the web: Background Sync API (browser support in 2026, esp. Safari/iOS), service worker outbox pattern, IndexedDB queues, Workbox background-sync, idempotency keys (Stripe-style) to make retries safe, handling auth token expiry while queued.
4. Child-friendly copy patterns for failure/rejection (encouraging, non-blaming).

Keep the report under ~700 words, organised by the 4 headings, ending with a short "recommendation for Jarso" paragraph that distinguishes what should queue offline vs stay online-only. Mark anything uncertain. Do not read any .env files.


[CLAUDE_LOG_ENTRY type=SUBAGENT_RESPONSE num=1 session=f88ac5af-0f6c-4ec8-b8bf-1fe3f91a8e7c agent=ace69f169610e68b7]
timestamp: 2026-10-30T03:37:35.667Z
time: 2026-10-30 03:37
chars: 6746

Report: what to do when a queued offline write is rejected later

**1. Prior art and UX patterns**
- **WhatsApp**: a message moves through clock (queued), one tick (sent), two ticks (delivered). If it fails, it shows a red "!" and "Message not sent. Tap to retry." The failed item stays in place with one recovery action. https://www.facebook.com/fb-answers/whatsapp-red-exclamation-mark-message-not-sent-tap-to-retry/
- **Gmail**: the Outbox labels unsent mail "Queued" and sends it when the connection returns. The item stays visible until it is sent. https://mailmeteor.com/blog/gmail-queued , https://support.google.com/mail/answer/1306849
- **Linear sync engine** (reverse-engineered, not official): writes are local "transactions" kept with the old values. If the server rejects one (conflict or permissions), the client rolls the change back, and the UI may flicker. Persisted transactions are sent when the app comes back online. https://github.com/wzhudev/reverse-linear-sync-engine
- **Electric and Jazz sync docs**: both say that if the server rejects an offline write, the app must revert local state and tell the user. Jazz separates a "local" write from a "server-confirmed" one, and only the local tier can resolve while offline. https://electric.ax/docs/sync/guides/writes , https://github.com/garden-co/jazz (docs/writing/writing-data.mdx)
- **Todoist**: its help page warns not to clear local data before sync finishes, because unsynced changes would be lost. https://www.todoist.com/help/todoist/troubleshooting/troubleshoot-syncing-issues-in-todoist-d6dDzzpF
- The common pattern has three parts: a visible "pending" state per item, a failed state that stays where the item is (not a toast that disappears), and a reason plus a choice of retry, edit or discard.
- **Not verified**: I found no primary source on how Monzo, Revolut, GoHenry, Greenlight or Rooster Money behave offline. My belief is that they block money movement when offline rather than queue it, but I could not confirm that.

**2. What is safe to queue vs online-only**
- OceanoBe (2026) on offline-first payments: "offline-first does not mean executing payments offline." The ledger, limits and checks live on the server. The client only keeps the user's intent and must report status honestly. Its states are queued, sending, pending confirmation, completed, rejected and expired. Each request gets an idempotency key, and the server enforces it with database constraints. https://oceanobe.com/news/offline-first-payments-in-react-native/2026
- General consensus: drafts, notes and journal-style entries can be queued safely. Anything that depends on balances, limits, another person's decision or authorization must be checked on the server at sync time. Results can differ from what the user saw, so the UI must treat them as provisional, not final. (This is a synthesis, not one source.)

**3. Technical mechanics on the web**
- **Background Sync API**: works in Chrome, Edge, Samsung Internet and Chrome Android. It does **not** work in Safari, Safari iOS or Firefox (MDN, and caniuse up to Safari 27.x). Because of this, iPhone users, likely a large share of 11–14s, get no background sending. https://caniuse.com/background-sync , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Background_Synchronization_API
- **Workbox `workbox-background-sync`**: stores failed POSTs in an IndexedDB `Queue`. Where the sync event is missing, it falls back to replaying the queue when the service worker next starts. In practice that means the next time the user opens the app, not true background sending. https://developer.chrome.com/docs/workbox/modules/workbox-background-sync
- **Practical pattern without a service worker**: keep a queue in IndexedDB and flush it on the `online` event, on app focus or visibility, and on app start.
- **Idempotency keys**: generate the key and **save it with the queued item before the first attempt**. A retry with the same key returns the stored result. A new key can repeat the action and double-apply it. Set a time limit on retries, then reconcile. https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests , https://stripe.com/blog/idempotency , https://dev.to/axrisi/idempotency-keys-in-stripe-safe-retries-after-lost-responses-5659
- **Expired login while queued**: no strong single source found. Common practice is to not store tokens in the queue. Attach the current session when replaying. On a 401, pause the queue, ask the user to sign in, then resume. The server must check authorization again at replay time, never trust what was checked when the item was queued.

**4. Child-friendly failure copy**
- NN/g: say what happened, give advice the user can act on, use a positive tone, and never blame the user. Avoid words like "invalid", "illegal" or "incorrect". Put responsibility on the system, and avoid humor in error messages. https://www.nngroup.com/articles/error-message-guidelines/ , https://www.nngroup.com/articles/error-messages-scoring-rubric/
- Children's UX: keep screens simple, use progressive disclosure, and match wording to the age group. https://www.nngroup.com/articles/childrens-websites-usability-issues/ , https://smart-interface-design-patterns.com/articles/design-guidelines-children/
- Applied example (my suggestion, not sourced): "We couldn't add your snack purchase. Your Spend jar has £1.20 left, and your rules don't allow going below zero. You can change the amount, ask a parent, or remove it." Keep what the child typed, and show one clear button for each choice.

**Recommendation for Jarso**
Queue offline only actions where the child is recording their own intent and the server can still decide, with no third party involved. That covers diary purchase entries, notes and mission or practice progress. Show each queued item as "Waiting to send". When it syncs, the server re-checks money rules, balances and authorization using a saved idempotency key. If the server rejects it, keep the entry in a "Needs a look" state with a short reason in plain words and three choices: edit, ask a parent, or remove. It must never silently disappear or silently debit the jar.

Keep **online-only**, with a clear "You're offline, try again when connected" message: anything a parent confirms (allowance handovers, gifts, approving savings requests), jar transfers, goal withdrawals, rule changes and sign-in or account actions. These depend on fresh balances or on the other parent's state, and a delayed rejection there causes confusion or real harm.

Don't rely on Background Sync, because it isn't available on iOS. Flush the queue when the app opens, regains focus or comes back online.

Unverified: how the named kids' fintech apps behave offline, and Safari's plans for Background Sync.

## 1. Next docs (node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app)
- **offline-support.md + 03-api-reference/05-config/01-next-config-js/useOffline.md**: turning on `experimental.useOffline: true` means failed navigations, RSC fetches, prefetches and Server Actions no longer throw. They stay pending and run again once the network is back. The `useOffline()` hook from `next/offline` returns false during SSR and hydration. While offline, Next polls a `HEAD` request with the RSC header, aborted at 200 ms, with backoff of 500 ms, 1 s, 2 s, then 3 s forever. A request still pending at 200 ms counts as "online", so a slow network looks online. A pending request runs once on reconnect. Caveats:
  - (a) Only a fetch that rejects with a non-abort, non-timeout error triggers offline mode. If the request reached the server but the response was lost, the action runs again on retry, so idempotency is required.
  - (b) The pending state lives only in memory. A reload or closed tab loses it, so there is no durable queue.
  - (c) A link click waits behind a pending action.
  - (d) It does not cover raw client `fetch` calls.
  - (e) Test with `next build && next start`, not dev.
  - With cacheComponents + partialPrefetching, the prefetched App Shell renders offline. Use `<Suspense fallback={<ConnectivityFallback/>}>` or a `loading.tsx` that calls `useOffline`.
- **progressive-web-apps.md**: manifest at `app/manifest.ts` (:20). Hand-written `public/sw.js` for push only (:551). Headers for sw.js: `Cache-Control: no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate` and a strict CSP (:636-666). For real offline caching it points to Serwist (:674).
- **use-link-status.md**: gives `{pending}` and must be used inside a `<Link>`. It skips the pending state when the route was already prefetched, and only the last clicked link shows pending. Mostly useful with `prefetch={false}`.
- **server-actions.md:26-32**: the client sends actions one at a time; the server does not queue them. At :174-180, a new deploy rotates action IDs, so old tabs get "Failed to find Server Action"; show it as a retry/refresh path. **maxDuration.md**: set at page level to change the timeout for every action on that page. The docs have no API to abort or time out a Server Action from the client.

## 2. Server Actions and idempotency
- `src/lib/action.ts:45-71` `createAction(schema, handler)`: Zod parse, AppError mapped to `ActionResult`, `unstable_rethrow`. **It has no generic idempotency key support**; keys are handled per action.
- Actions are in `src/features/{diary,allowance,family,auth,learning,rules,profiles,guide}/server/actions.ts`. RPC calls are in each repository.
- **Safe on retry:**
  - `record_purchase`: key from the client (`purchase-sheet.tsx:43`, one per sheet open), stored as `purchase:<child>:<key>` on the unique `ledger_entries.idempotency_key` (`20261029130001_ledger.sql:44`). Takes an advisory lock and returns the original entry on retry (`20261029183422_purchases.sql:166-175`).
  - `confirm_handover` / `confirm_gift_handover`: lock the row and check state (`due`/`pending`, otherwise `already_confirmed`/`not_given`/`cancelled`). Ledger keys come from the occurrence or gift (`occurrence:<id>:spend`). The client key is only checked for being non-blank and is not stored (`20261029161729_handover_closed_results.sql:20-27,82`).
  - `mark_not_given`: returns `already_handled` (`20261029130004_handover.sql`).
  - `undo_purchase`: `already_undone`.
  - `set_money_rule` / `set_allow_negative_spend`: upsert.
  - Learning `start_mission` (returns the open attempt), `record_step_result` (PK attempt/step/phase), `complete_mission`, `record_practice_result` (unique child/item/day), `complete_checkin` (on conflict do nothing).
- **Retry gives a misleading error (no duplicate):**
  - `cancel_gift`, `update_gift`, `pause`/`stop`/`resume_allowance`: raise JA409 when the state is no longer as expected.
  - `create_allowance`: unique "one live per child", so a retry gets JA409 "already has an allowance".
  - `create_family`: JA409 "Already in a family".
  - `addChild` (`family/server/service.ts:118-122`): creates the auth user, then `register_child`. A retry hits the nickname unique index ("taken") and deletes the orphan auth user.
- **Duplicates on retry:**
  - **`create_gift`** (`20261029130005_parent_gifts.sql:89-133`): plain INSERT, no key, no unique constraint. A double submit creates two pending gifts, and later two ledger postings.
  - **`askGuide`** (`guide/server/service.ts:110-160`): spends rate-limit hits, may call `guide_start_conversation` again, makes the Claude calls again and records a duplicate turn. The schema has no client message id (`guide/schemas.ts:30`).
  - `create_invitation` and the sign-up/sign-in actions are not idempotent; for auth that is acceptable.

## 3. Browser Supabase client
`src/lib/supabase/client.ts` is **not imported anywhere**. There is no `onAuthStateChange`, no `.channel()`, no realtime and no client subscriptions, so no leak risk today.

## 4. Sessions
- Token refresh happens only in `src/proxy.ts` through `updateSession` (`src/lib/supabase/proxy.ts:12-38`, `getClaims`). Settings: `jwt_expiry=3600`, refresh rotation on, `refresh_token_reuse_interval=10` (`supabase/config.toml:166-175`). Concurrent requests (prefetch storm or retry burst) with the same refresh token more than 10 s apart, for example a long-idle tab on a slow network, would trip reuse detection and sign the user out.
- `getCurrentUser` (`features/auth/server/session.ts:14-27`) checks the JWT locally. A revoked session or deleted user still passes `getClaims` until the access token expires (up to 1 h). `revoke_user_sessions` says so itself (`20261029013254_harden_onboarding.sql:26,32`). RLS and the membership-status checks are what actually block a removed child.
- `signOut` (`auth/server/actions.ts:55-59`) uses the default scope and ignores its error. If the network fails mid-request the cookie may survive.
- Child sign-in (`family/server/service.ts:287-290`): `rotateChildPassword` then `mintChildSession` (`child-auth.ts:79-93`: admin `generateLink` magiclink, then `verifyOtp` on the cookie client). A retry after a lost response rotates and mints again, which is harmless. GoTrue may end other child sessions on rotation. `setChildPin` rotates and revokes (:295-298). `removeChild` revokes, then deletes (:312+).

## 5. Long-running / background work
- Guide: `guide/server/claude.ts:28,82` uses `TIMEOUT_MS=20_000` and `maxRetries:1`, and replies are not streamed (:18). `pipeline.ts:111-150` runs the input screen and main reply in parallel, then an output screen. The AbortController only cancels main when the screen routes elsewhere or fails. **There is no abort on client disconnect** (Server Actions get no request signal). The worst case is about 3 × 20 s × 2 tries, roughly 2 min, and **no `maxDuration` is set anywhere**.
- `breaker.ts` keeps a per-instance in-memory circuit breaker (5 failures/min opens it for 60 s). The provider singleton is at `config.ts:23`.
- `notifications/server/email.ts:23` uses `AbortSignal.timeout(10_000)` and is awaited inline (`family/service.ts:158`).
- No `after()`, `setInterval` or `setTimeout` on the server. Route handlers are only `src/app/api/health/route.ts` (static ok) and `api/v1/me`. Cron runs in-DB via pg_cron (`20261029130003_allowance_generation.sql:110`, `20261030120000_guide.sql:406`). `vercel.json` sets only the region.

## 6. Boundaries / pending UI
- Only `src/app/error.tsx`, `global-error.tsx` and `not-found.tsx` exist. **There is no `loading.tsx`.** 32 files under `src/app` use `<Suspense>`.
- 20 files use `useActionState`. `useTransition` is used in undo-button, overspend-switch, rule-row, handover-form, allowance-summary, gift-form, refresh-invitations-button, mission-player, guide-switch and guide-sheet. `useFormStatus` is not used. `Button loading` disables the button (`components/ui/button.tsx:61`).
- `mission-player.tsx:68-77` wraps action rejections into "Check your connection".
- No `useOffline`, `useLinkStatus` or `navigator.onLine`. No manifest and no service worker (`public/` has only `brand/`). `next.config.ts` has no `useOffline` and no sw headers.

## 7. Helpers
There is no shared fetch-with-timeout, retry or timing helper. Timeouts are written inline (email, Anthropic). `src/lib/logger.ts` writes structured JSON with no timing. The guide records `latencyMs` by hand (`pipeline.ts:103-107`).

Questions to answer before planning, most important first.

1. **What should happen when Next.js holds an online-only action and sends it later?**
   With `experimental.useOffline` on, a Server Action called while offline does not fail. Next.js keeps it pending and sends it again by itself when the connection returns. The guide is `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/offline-support.md`, section "Retry Server Actions after the network returns".
   - Online-only actions (handover, undo purchase, gift and allowance changes, sign-out): the spec says they "don't send" when offline. Checking `useOffline()` before the call only stops taps made after Next.js has noticed the drop. A tap during the gap, or on a slow network that Next.js counts as online, gets held and goes through silently minutes later.
     - Is that late send acceptable?
     - Or must these actions be cancelled and show the warning instead? That would mean calling them some other way than a plain Server Action, or wrapping them with a client-side abort and timeout.
   - The device queue: if it sends through Server Actions, it never sees a network failure. The call just hangs inside Next.js's in-memory retry. So the "network/timeout stays `waiting` with backoff" rule can't work as written, and a hung call blocks the one-at-a-time order.
     - Does the decision-maker accept a `withApi` route under `api/v1` for the queue as the default, not a fallback?
     - Or should the flag be turned on in a way that leaves the queue's calls alone?
   - The answer changes the transport layer, the error mapping and the e2e tests.

2. **Which time does a queued item carry: when the child did it, or when it reached the server?**
   - Today `record_purchase` sets `occurred_at = now()`. A `record_purchase_at(p_now)` variant exists but isn't callable from the client path.
   - The learning RPCs (`record_practice_result`, `complete_checkin`, `start_mission`'s 24 h limit) all use the server's `family_today()` or `now()`.
   - If an item queued on Monday is sent on Tuesday:
     - the purchase lands on the wrong diary day;
     - it counts toward the wrong spending-limit window, so parent notices are wrong;
     - it gets a fresh "undo within a day" window;
     - the check-in can collide with Tuesday's real one, and "on conflict" silently drops one of them.
   - If the device time should be used, the plan needs:
     - new RPC parameters in a migration;
     - a trust and clamp rule (how far back, never in the future);
     - Zod fields in the payload.
   - If server time is fine, none of that is needed.

3. **Is opening the app with no network at all in scope?**
   - The Next.js docs say offline retry only covers soft navigations and Server Actions from a page that is already loaded. A full reload, or opening from the home screen, while offline gets the browser's offline page without a Service Worker.
   - Goal 2 says items survive "reloads and closed tabs", and the spec says "No Service Worker … required". Those fit only if the child can't add a purchase after a cold start offline, and queued items just send the next time the app loads online.
   - If a child on patchy school Wi-Fi must be able to open the diary and record a purchase from a cold start, the plan needs a Service Worker or PWA shell cache. That is significant extra scope and testing.

4. **What does "ask a parent" on a "Needs a look" item actually do?**
   - Today "ask a parent" is only advice in the copy (`purchase-sheet.tsx`). There is no child-to-parent request for purchases.
   - If it should notify the parent, the plan needs a new notification kind, the payload and parent-side UI. The `notifications.kind` check constraint means that's a migration.
   - If it's just advice and the item stays parked, it's a small UI-only task.

5. **Who changes the production Supabase Auth setting, and when?**
   - `refresh_token_reuse_interval` is a setting in the hosted project's dashboard. The builder can mirror it in `supabase/config.toml` but can't apply it to production from the repo.
   - Knowing whether someone with dashboard access will make the change, and in what order relative to the deploy, decides whether the plan needs a code-side fallback for the sign-out-after-idle problem or only a runbook step.

1. [Important, spec] Timeouts and network failures are not logged (src/lib/timed-fetch.ts catch path). Log `logger.warn("Upstream call failed", { name, path, durationMs, reason: "timeout" | "network" })` (no query string, no payload) and add tests for both.

2. [Important, controller-verified] JA503 never reaches toAppError. postgrest-js 2.118 (node_modules/.pnpm/@supabase+postgrest-js@2.118.0/.../dist/index.cjs:420-455) converts ANY fetch rejection into `{ code: "" , message: "<name>: <message>" }`, so the `.code` on TimedFetchError is discarded. It also retries GET requests up to 3 times on rejection and on status 503 (index.cjs:79, 199-224), which would turn an 8 s timeout into ~40 s. Controller ruling: on timeout or network failure, the timed fetch must RESOLVE a synthetic `Response` with status 503, header `content-type: application/json`, body `{"code":"JA503","message":"Jarso is taking a while. Please try again."}` instead of rejecting. A caller-initiated abort still rejects with the AbortError unchanged. Set `db: { retry: false }` on all three Supabase clients (server.ts, proxy.ts, admin.ts; supabase-js 2.118 supports `db.retry`). auth-js already treats a 503 response as AuthRetryableFetchError (auth-js dist/main/lib/fetch.js:38-53), so session.ts's `isAuthRetryableFetchError` guard still holds — keep it, and drop the `TimedFetchError` cause branch if it becomes dead code (remove `TimedFetchError`/`isTimedFetchError` entirely if nothing needs them; update the brief's names in your report). Tests: `resolves a 503 JA503 response after timeoutMs`, `resolves a 503 JA503 response on a network TypeError`, `caller abort still rejects`, plus one integration-style test building a real `createClient` from `@supabase/supabase-js` with `global.fetch` = a timed fetch over a never-resolving stub and `db.retry: false`, asserting `await client.rpc("x")` yields `error.code === "JA503"` after one timeout (fake timers), and that `toAppError(error, "…").code === "UNAVAILABLE"`.

3. [Important] ProfileGate must stay fail-open (src/app/(app)/profile-gate.tsx:18): `getCurrentUser()` and `currentRole()` now can throw UNAVAILABLE outside its try, which would replace the whole page with error.tsx. Move them inside the existing try (keep the warn log). Add/extend a test if a ProfileGate test exists; otherwise a small one asserting it returns null when getCurrentUser throws.

4. [Minor, do it] Add a one-line comment in timed-fetch.ts that the deadline covers the time to response headers, not the body read (Supabase bodies are small).

Address this before completing your current task.

timestamp: 2026-10-30T06:10:57.726Z
time: 2026-10-30 06:10
chars: 4885

### Spec Compliance
- ❌ Two gaps against the brief:
  - **Timeouts and network failures are not logged.** The brief says "If the duration exceeds `slowMs` … logs `logger.warn`". `src/lib/timed-fetch.ts:638-648` logs only on the success path. A call that hits the 8 s deadline (the slowest case) or fails on the network (the `catch` at 649-652) writes nothing from this layer. The test suite has no case for it. The brief's `status` field has no value for these cases, so the spec is ambiguous here. Even so, the incident that motivated this task is invisible unless a downstream layer logs it.
  - **Deviation from the brief, which I accept.** The brief says to combine with `AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs)`. The code uses `AbortController` + `setTimeout`, joined via `AbortSignal.any` (`timed-fetch.ts:632-634`). The reason given (native `AbortSignal.timeout` is not driven by fake timers) is plausible, and the timer is cleared in `finally`. Observable behaviour is the same, and cleanup is better.
- ✅ Everything else matches the brief.
  - Constants: 8000 ms timeout, 1000 ms slow threshold, exact `UNAVAILABLE` message.
  - Error plumbing: the `JA503` mapping, `UNAVAILABLE: 503`, and the barrel export.
  - Client wiring: `supabaseFetch` is on all three clients.
  - Auth path: the `getCurrentUser` throw, the `updateSession` `unavailable` flag, and the early return in `src/proxy.ts`.
  - Logging: logs carry name, path without query, status and duration only. There are no payloads or keys.
- ⚠️ Cannot verify from the diff:
  - I could not confirm that postgrest-js propagates a `.code` of `JA503` from a rejected fetch into its `error` object. The `JA503` → `UNAVAILABLE` mapping only works if it does. My grep of the installed package found nothing (the glob failed), so I did not confirm it either way. Task 2 or later should have an integration check for this.
  - I could not confirm that auth-js wraps the `TimedFetchError` as `AuthRetryableFetchError`. The `isAuthRetryableFetchError` guard relies on that, and the unit test only simulates it.

### Strengths
- The caller-abort path is handled correctly. It is detected via `init.signal.aborted` and rethrown unwrapped (`timed-fetch.ts:650`), and a test covers it.
- The timer is cleared in `finally`, so there is no leaked timer on success, failure or abort.
- `AbortSignal.any` only runs when a caller signal exists, which avoids needless listener wiring.
- `pathnameOf` strips the query and falls back to `"unknown"` on a bad URL. The test asserts the query is gone.
- The module is isomorphic, with no env access and no `console`.
- The `unavailable` flag in `src/lib/supabase/proxy.ts` and the early return in `src/proxy.ts` stop a slow network from redirecting users to login. The comments explain why.
- The extra session tests (signed out, user from claims) check real behaviour, and the `cache` mock is explained.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **Timeouts and network failures leave no log** (`src/lib/timed-fetch.ts:649-652`).
   - Emit `logger.warn` (or `error`) with `{ name, path, durationMs, reason }` before throwing `TimedFetchError`, and add a test.
   - Callers that convert the error to `AppError("UNAVAILABLE")` may not log it. The `getCurrentUser` and proxy paths added here do not.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **The deadline stops when headers arrive.** `clearTimeout` in `finally` (`timed-fetch.ts:653-655`) runs once `fetch` resolves, so a stalled body read has no deadline.
   - The Supabase JSON bodies are small, so this is low risk.
   - The caller signal still applies, but the deadline does not.
   - Either document it or keep the timer running until the body is consumed.
2. **A `Request` input loses its own signal** (`timed-fetch.ts:638`). `{ ...init, signal }` replaces any signal carried by a `Request` passed as `input`. Supabase passes URL strings, so this is theoretical.
3. **`ProfileGate` now throws on a timeout.** `src/app/(app)/profile-gate.tsx:18` calls `getCurrentUser()` outside its fail-open `try`. A timeout now throws `UNAVAILABLE` there, where it used to return null. This is probably intended and later tasks can handle it, but the "fails open" docstring no longer fully holds.
   - The same change reaches `getMembership` and `currentRole` in `family/server/dal.ts`: the throw propagates and the cached `getCurrentUser` rejection is shared for the request.
4. **No test for the combined-signal listener path or for timer cleanup.** Adding one would harden the behaviour.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The implementation is correct and follows the brief; the one defensible deviation (`AbortController` + `setTimeout`) is sound. I recommend a quick follow-up to log timeouts and network failures, because they are the incident this work targets and the layer currently stays silent about them.

### Spec Compliance
- Pipeline deadline: MET. `pipeline.ts` builds one `AbortSignal.any([AbortSignal.timeout(deadlineMs), controller.signal])` and passes it to reply, screenInput and screenOutput. `deadlineMs` defaults to 45_000 via `options?: { deadlineMs }`. This matches the brief and the global 45 000 ms constraint.
- Provider contract: MET. `signal?` was added to `screenInput` and `screenOutput` in `provider.ts`, and `claude.ts` forwards `{ signal }` to all three `messages.parse` calls.
- Deadline result: MET. A deadline abort makes the call throw. That reaches the existing catch blocks, which return `fallbackResult(..., true)` or `fixed("unavailable", ..., true)`, so `failed: true`. The test asserts `route: "unavailable"`, `failed: true`, resolution within 500 ms, and that every signal received was aborted.
- (a) maxDuration coverage: MISSING on two pages.
  - `src/app/(app)/child/page.tsx:18` and `src/app/(app)/child/learn/topics/[topicKey]/page.tsx:14` got `maxDuration = 60`.
  - `src/app/(app)/child/learn/[missionKey]/page.tsx:51` and `src/app/(app)/child/learn/practice/page.tsx:51` both render `<StepGuide>`. `step-guide.tsx:24` renders `GuideSheet`, which is what sends the guide action.
  - Neither page has `maxDuration` (grep of `src` shows only the two pages above). A guide turn on those pages can run the full 45 s plus the save, past the platform default.
  - The controller ruling only exempts read-only parent transcript pages. These two are not exempt.
  - The brief's `grep -rl GuideSheet src/app` would not have found them, because they import `StepGuide`. The brief's own wording ("every page that can trigger askGuideAction") still covers them.
- (b) removed `now` parameter: MET. In `src` the only caller is `service.ts:135`, which passed two args. In `evals/guide/guide.eval.ts:107` the call also passes two args. Neither relied on the old parameter. Inside `pipeline.ts`, `const now = Date.now` keeps the latency code intact.
- (c) breaker and half-done writes: MET. `service.ts:136` records a failure whenever `result.failed` is true, so a deadline-aborted turn feeds the breaker. The failed result goes through the same `recordTurn` path as any other provider failure, and the pipeline writes nothing itself.
- `service.ts` was listed under "Modify" in the brief but is unchanged. This is fine, since it needed no change.
- ⚠️ Not checked: whether Next 16 with Cache Components accepts `export const maxDuration` in a page file. The implementer was asked to verify this. I did not inspect it, and the diff alone can't show it.
- ⚠️ Not checked: the Vercel Hobby limit note for the commit body. I did not inspect the commit message.

### Strengths
- A single shared signal covers all three provider calls, so a deadline or the internal cancel stops all of them. The change is small and sits inside the existing pipeline structure.
- The Anthropic SDK's `APIUserAbortError` extends `APIError` (confirmed in `core/error.d.ts:19`). The `parsed()` helper in `claude.ts` rethrows `APIError`, so an abort surfaces as a throw and is not swallowed into a `null`. If it were swallowed, the input screen would route to "safety" on a timeout instead of "unavailable". This path works correctly.
- The deadline test uses real short timers (50 ms) and asserts behaviour: elapsed time, result flags, and that all signals were aborted.
- Only a signal, not timer handles, is created. `AbortSignal.timeout` is unref'd and cleaned up by the runtime, so there are no manual timers or listeners to leak.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. `maxDuration = 60` is missing from `src/app/(app)/child/learn/[missionKey]/page.tsx` and `src/app/(app)/child/learn/practice/page.tsx`. Both render `StepGuide`, which wraps `GuideSheet`. A guide turn started from either page can exceed the default function duration. This breaks the global constraint "guide pages `maxDuration = 60`".

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. `pipeline.ts`: the deadline and the internal cancel are indistinguishable in logs. Both surface as an abort error name in "Guide main call failed" or "Guide input screen failed". After the input screen routes a message away from on-topic, `controller.abort()` cancels the main call, but that error is never logged, so this is harmless today. Still, deadline hits are not labelled, which will make production diagnosis harder. Consider logging `signal.reason` or a `deadline` flag.
2. `pipeline.test.ts` "passes one deadline signal to the output screen too" is weak. It only checks that a signal is defined and not aborted. It would pass even if the screens got different signals. Asserting `screenSignal === replySignal`, or that the input screen got the same signal, would pin the "one shared deadline" claim.
3. The `parsed()` doc comment in `claude.ts` still says only "non-HTTP error becomes null". It could mention that aborts rethrow through `APIUserAbortError`, since correct behaviour depends on that class hierarchy.
4. `.claude-logs` churn is expected per project rules, so I treated it as out of scope.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** The deadline logic is correct and well tested, and the breaker path and eval caller are intact. Two child pages that render `StepGuide` (`learn/[missionKey]` and `learn/practice`) still lack `maxDuration = 60`, so a guide turn there can exceed the function duration. Adding the export to both pages fixes it.

   - `src/features/guide/server/pipeline.ts:348` keeps `deadline = AbortSignal.timeout(deadlineMs)` separate from the internal controller. Line 349 combines them.
   - `pipeline.ts:350-356`: `noteDeadline()` returns unless `deadline.aborted` is true and `deadlineLogged` is false. It then sets the flag and calls `logger.warn("Guide turn hit its deadline", { deadlineMs })`. Message text is not logged.
   - Call sites: `pipeline.ts:371` (input screen catch), `:384` (main call failed), `:406` (output screen catch).
   - The internal `controller.abort()` at `:370`, `:378` does not set `deadline.aborted`, so an internal cancel never logs. Calling `controller.abort()` before `noteDeadline()` at `:370` is harmless for the same reason.
   - The `deadlineLogged` guard holds the once-per-turn limit.

3. [Minor] Test asserts one shared signal: ADDRESSED.
   - `src/features/guide/server/pipeline.test.ts:294-322` ("passes the same deadline signal to every provider call") records the signal from `screenInput`, `reply` and `screenOutput`.
   - `:319` asserts input is defined. `:320-321` assert `reply` and `output` are `toBe(signals.input)`, which is identity, not equality.

### New Breakage in Fix Diff

None at Critical or Important.

- Deadline log fires at most once per turn: confirmed by the `deadlineLogged` guard.
- No message text logged: confirmed. The log carries only `deadlineMs`. The existing `errorName(...)` lines are unchanged.
- Minor edge, not a defect: `noteDeadline()` checks `deadline.aborted` at the moment a failure is observed. A non-deadline error that lands after the deadline has already fired would be labelled as a deadline hit. Only the log label is affected, not the route or the `failed` flag.

timestamp: 2026-10-30T06:33:26.610Z
time: 2026-10-30 06:33
chars: 7290

### Spec Compliance
- Gift column, backfill, constraints: match the brief. `supabase/migrations/20261030130000_retry_safe_gift.sql:16-22` adds the column, backfills with `gen_random_uuid()::text`, then sets `not null`, a length check and `unique (created_by, idempotency_key)`. Doing all of this in one migration is correct. The table is `parent_gifts`, as the controller ruled.
- Old 4-arg `create_gift`: dropped (`:26`). The new 5-arg function has security definer, `search_path=''`, and its revoke/grant repeated (`:113-114`). I grepped src, supabase and tests: the only callers are the repository (`src/features/allowance/server/repository.ts:288`), the pgTAP files and `tests/e2e/handover-race.spec.ts:110`, all updated. The remaining hits in `20261029130005_parent_gifts.sql:366,374` are historical grant lists in an earlier migration, so not a caller. (d) OK.
- Guide column and index: `client_message_id` plus the partial unique index are present (`20261030130001_retry_safe_guide_turn.sql:130-140`). It adds an extra check, child rows only, which is a sensible extra. The 11-arg `guide_record_turn` stores the id on the child row. The 10-arg version is dropped and the grants are repeated (`:256-259`).
- (a) `guide_find_turn` grants: revoked from public, anon and authenticated; granted to service_role (`:257,259`). Both directions are tested (`retry_safety.test.sql:56-59`). The service passes `user.id` from `assertRole`, never a client value (`service.ts` askGuide, `storedTurn(admin, user.id, …)`). The reply is loaded through the RLS user client. OK.
- pgTAP: all four required tests are present (`retry_safety.test.sql`). Unit tests: both required names are present (`allowance/server/service.test.ts` "forwards the idempotency key"; `guide/server/service.test.ts` asserts `hitRateLimit` and the provider screenInput/reply/screenOutput were never called).
- Zod: `idempotencyKey: z.uuid()` and `clientMessageId: z.uuid()` added (`allowance/schemas.ts`, `guide/schemas.ts`).
- (e) Client keys:
  - `gift-form.tsx`: one key from `useState(() => crypto.randomUUID())` per mount, sent as a hidden input on create only.
  - `guide-sheet.tsx`: the `unanswered` ref keeps `{text, id}`. The id is reused when the same text is re-sent, and cleared on success and on reset.
  - The existing unit tests, both pgTAP files and the handover-race e2e were all updated. `randomUUID` is already imported in that spec.
- (b) Deviation from named risk (b): the replay runs before the guide-access checks. In `src/features/guide/server/service.ts` askGuide, `storedTurn` is called right after `assertRole`, before `getGuideProvider()` (the deployment switch) and `findGuideAccess` (the parent switch). So a re-send after the parent turns the guide off still returns the stored reply. This matches the brief's literal "askGuide first calls repo.findTurn", but not the controller's stated requirement. See Minor 1 for severity.
- (f) Concurrent race loser: handled. In the `recordTurn` catch block, `storedTurn(...).catch(() => null)` runs first, so the loser of a 23505 gets the winner's turn instead of an error. The unit test "answers with the stored turn when the same question raced in" covers it. Lookups made after a unique violation see the committed winner, so this is sound.
- (c) Gift replay with the same key but a different amount or date silently returns the original gift id (`retry_safe_gift.sql:65-71`). The lookup runs before amount/date validation and does not compare the payload or `child_id`. This is not tested. It is acceptable here because the key is per form mount with a fixed `childId`, but it is weaker than Stripe, which rejects a reused key with different parameters.
- Accepted deviations: two migrations (ruled OK) and hand-renamed migration timestamps. The renaming is needed because the generator's stamp would sort before `20261030120000_guide.sql`, and it is reported honestly.

### Strengths
- `create_gift` handles the race correctly: lookup, then `insert … on conflict do nothing returning`, then read back the winner's row. The audit row is written only when a row is inserted, and a test checks there is one audit entry.
- Skipping the date re-check on replay is deliberate: a retry after midnight still finds the original gift.
- Authz runs before the replay lookup, and the replay is scoped to `created_by = auth.uid()`. The cross-parent case is tested.
- `guide_find_turn` is tightly scoped: service_role only, filtered by child, and it works because the child row and reply share one `clock_timestamp()` value.
- The guide unit tests check the full replay result and that the provider, rate limiter, startConversation and recordTurn were never called. There is also a negative test: a failed save with no stored turn still fails.
- Layering holds: only the repositories name tables and RPCs. No new log carries message text or keys.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **The replay bypasses the guide on/off switches (named risk b).** Location: `src/features/guide/server/service.ts` askGuide, where `storedTurn` comes before `getGuideProvider`/`findGuideAccess`.
   - Real impact is low. I checked the RLS policies in the guide migration: a child can always read their own `guide_messages` (`is_self(child_id)`), and the replay makes no model call and adds no cost. So nothing new is exposed.
   - It still contradicts the controller's stated rule. Moving the replay below the `findGuideAccess` check, but still above context, conversation and rate limit, keeps every brief guarantee and meets (b). Raise to Important if the controller treats (b) as binding.
2. **A gift replay ignores the payload and `child_id`.** Location: `20261030130000_retry_safe_gift.sql:65-71`. A same-key call with a different amount, date or child returns the old id, and the UI then shows "Gift saved". The UI can't reach this today. Consider either comparing `child_id` or documenting the behaviour. No test pins it either way.
3. **A benign race is logged as an error.** When a concurrent duplicate hits 23505, `toAppError` logs `logger.error("Database call failed", …)` (`src/lib/db-error.ts:31`) before the service recovers. That is noise in the error log for a handled path. It carries no secrets: the PG message is a constraint name only.
4. **The server doesn't check that the re-sent text matches.** A replayed `clientMessageId` returns the stored reply whatever `input.text` says. The client only reuses an id for identical text, so this is a server-trust gap with no real-world consequence.
5. **Every `recordTurn` failure costs extra queries.** On any failure, including JA409 "chat full", the catch now runs `findTurn` (and `listMessages` on a hit). The cost is negligible, but a comment saying the check applies to every failure would make that clear.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** Every interface, migration, grant, test and client-key requirement in the brief is met and checked in the diff. The race paths for gifts and for the guide are handled correctly. The one point against the controller's named risk (b), the replay running before the guide-access checks, exposes nothing the child can't already read under RLS, so it is a small, optional reorder.

### Findings

1. **Guide replay ordering: ADDRESSED.**
   - In `src/features/guide/server/service.ts` askGuide, the order is now `assertRole`, then `getGuideProvider()` (deployment check), then `findGuideAccess` (parent check), then `createAdminClient()` and `storedTurn` replay. Context building, conversation lookup, rate-limit hits and provider calls all come after the replay (`getFamily`, `buildContext` and the rest follow it).
   - The required test exists in `src/features/guide/server/service.test.ts` with the exact name. It sets `findGuideAccess` to false and expects FORBIDDEN with `guideCopy.switchedOff`. It also asserts that `findTurn`, `listMessages`, `screenInput` and `reply` are not called. The `findTurn` mock resolves to a stored turn, so the test fails if the replay runs before the access check.
   - I did not check that `getGuideProvider` is mocked to return a provider in the test setup (`spiedProvider()`). If it were not, the test would pass for the wrong reason, because the deployment check would throw first.

2. **Gift replay scoped to child: ADDRESSED.**
   - `supabase/migrations/20261030130000_retry_safe_gift.sql:65-71` now matches `child_id = p_child_id` in the replay lookup.
   - The required pgTAP test is in `supabase/tests/database/retry_safety.test.sql`. It uses a second child, `c2`, registered in the fixture, and calls `create_gift` with the first child's key. The plan is bumped from 14 to 15 to match. The test is named `same key for a different child does not return the first child's gift` and expects `JA409`.

### Judging the JA409 choice

- **Correct.** The unique constraint is `(created_by, idempotency_key)`, so a new insert for a different child would hit the constraint. `on conflict do nothing` would then return null and the insert would silently do nothing. A reused key with different parameters is a client bug, and rejecting it (as Stripe does) is better than returning the wrong gift or dropping the request.
- **Race path:** the read-back after `on conflict do nothing` also matches `child_id = p_child_id`. If that returns null, it raises `JA409`. Two concurrent same-key, same-child sends still return the winner's id. A concurrent send for a different child raises `JA409`.
- **User-facing message:** the DB message is "Key already used for another gift". I did not check how the repository maps `JA409` to an `AppError`, because that is outside the diff. The case cannot be reached from the UI: `gift-form.tsx` generates one key per mount with a fixed `childId`. The message therefore only matters as a defensive error.
- **Documentation:** the function `comment on` and `docs/database.md` are both updated to match.

### New Breakage in Fix Diff

None.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- The reused-key `JA409` is raised after amount and date validation. A reused key with an invalid amount therefore gets `22023` instead of `JA409`. This is harmless.
- I did not check how `JA409` from the gift repository is mapped to a message (see above).

### Verdict

All findings addressed: yes

### Spec Compliance
- `resolve_occurred_at` matches the brief: null gives now(); more than 60 s ahead or more than 7 days back raises 22023; otherwise `least(p, now())` (migration `20261030130002_device_occurred_at.sql:11-33`).
- `record_purchase(…, p_occurred_at default null)` is a SQL wrapper that calls `record_purchase_at(…, resolve_occurred_at(p))`. The old 4-arg signature is dropped, execute is revoked from public/anon and re-granted to authenticated (migration :41-66). Pass.
- `complete_mission(p_attempt_id, p_occurred_at)`: `completed_at` and the review day both come from `family_today(v_family_id, v_at)`. Dropped and re-granted (migration :74-118). Pass.
- `record_practice_result(…, p_occurred_at)`: I diffed the new body against the old one in `20261030100000_learning_progress.sql`. The only changes are the `v_at`/`v_today` declarations and `answered_at = v_at`. Nothing was lost. Pass.
- Controller rulings are followed. `family_day_at` was not added; `family_today(uuid, timestamptz default now())` already exists (`20261029130002_allowance_schedules.sql:107`). `start_mission`, `record_step_result` and `complete_checkin` are unchanged.
- TS: the three schemas gain `occurredAt: z.iso.datetime({ offset: true }).optional()`. Services, repositories and `completeMissionAction` forward it. `recordPracticeAction` passes the parsed input whole (`learning/server/actions.ts:37`), so it gets the field too. The repositories pass `undefined` rather than `?? null`. The generated type is `p_occurred_at?: string`, so this is a justified deviation with the same effect.
- All 7 pgTAP cases the brief asked for are present (`purchases.test.sql` +8, `learning_progress.test.sql` +6). So are all 4 unit tests.
- (d) Old arities: the only callers in src are the three repository rpc calls, all on the new arity. SQL tests call with positional args and still resolve through the default. No hits in tests/e2e or evals. A `drop function` would have failed if anything depended on the old signature, and the report says `db reset` passed. Pass.
- (e) `resolve_occurred_at` is stable, security invoker, `search_path=''` and pure apart from now(). Stable is correct, because now() is fixed for the transaction and raising is allowed in a stable function. Execute is revoked from public/anon/authenticated, with a pgTAP 42501 check. service_role keeps it, which does no harm. The callers are all security definer, so they run it as the owner. Pass.
- ⚠️ I did not re-run the RED/GREEN output; I took the report's figures as claims.

### Strengths
- The change reuses the existing `record_purchase_at` clock seam. Ledger time, the spend-limit window, the notice key and the undo window all follow the action time with no new branching, and the balance check still reads the current `balance()`/`held_spend()`.
- Replay tests are good: same key with a different backdated time returns the original entry, writes one row and keeps the original time. A refused time writes nothing.
- The migration comments and the function `comment on` text were updated, along with docs/database.md.

### Named-risk checks
- **(a1) Backdating past a spending limit.** I read `evaluate_spend_limits` (`20261029183422_purchases.sql:32-113`). Spend limits only send notices; they never refuse a purchase. Backdating cannot let a child spend more money, because the Spend check uses the current balance. The only abuse is hiding a notice. Example with a 500/week limit: 450 spent this week, then a 300 purchase backdated into last week, which was empty. That window ends at 300 and this week stays at 450, so the parent gets no notice. If the target window then goes over the limit, it gets its own notice under that window's key. Severity is low: this is a self-reported diary, and a child can already hide spending by not logging it. Minor, and the parent's diary view should show `occurred_at` honestly.
- **(a2) Purchase before join date or before Spend had money.** This is possible within 7 days. The membership check uses current approved status, and the balance is current. The only effect is a diary row dated before joining or before the first allowance. `list_ledger_history` orders by `occurred_at` with no running balance (`purchases.sql:347`), so no negative historical balance is ever shown. Cosmetic.
- **(a3) Undo direction.** `undo_purchase_at` returns too_late when `occurred_at < now - 24h` (`purchases.sql:270`). Backdating shortens the undo window and never lengthens it. Confirmed safe. One UX consequence: a purchase queued more than 24 h ago cannot be undone at all once it syncs.
- **(b) resolve before replay.** Confirmed: the wrapper evaluates `resolve_occurred_at` before `record_purchase_at` runs its auth checks and replay lookup. The outbox measures 7 days from creation on the device clock, while the server measures from the device-stamped time on the server clock. If the device clock runs behind by δ, an item younger than 7 days on the device can look older than 7 days to the server. A legitimate retry inside that gap gets 22023 instead of the original entry, even though the entry exists. Task 6 maps 22023 to "needs a look", so a child may re-enter the purchase by hand and create a duplicate. It needs about 7 days offline plus clock skew or a lost response near the boundary, so it is rare. Minor.
- **(c) Extras.** Both extras are consistent with the spec ("follows the action time"). The clamp (`completed_at` ≥ `started_at`, migration :100) guards a device clock that runs behind, and it is sensible because `start_mission` is online-only. Setting `answered_at = v_at` keeps it consistent with `answered_on`. The streak / once-a-day check found no streak logic and no other `completed_at` consumers. Neither extra is unwanted scope.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. `migration:41-60`: an idempotent replay is refused once the device time ages past 7 days (risk b). Fix options: do the replay lookup before the range check (for example, pass the raw time into a seam and validate it after the lookup), or have Task 6 avoid prompting a re-entry for a 22023 on an item that was already sent.
2. Backdating into a past window can hide a spend-limit notice (risk a1). Low severity because the diary is self-reported. Consider noting it in docs/domain.md as an accepted trade-off.
3. `migration:84, 156`: `resolve_occurred_at` runs in the `declare` block, so a bad time returns 22023 before the JA403 or not-a-child checks in complete_mission and record_practice_result. Nothing leaks; it is only an inconsistent error order.
4. Test gaps:
   - Nothing covers the `greatest(v_at, started_at)` clamp (a completion stamped before the start).
   - Nothing shows that a backdated purchase lands in the past spend-limit window, which is the spec's stated intent. The seam tests cover it only indirectly.
5. Report inaccuracy: the table says "Idempotency lookup runs before everything". It runs after the time check and the auth/validation checks; the report's own Concerns section contradicts the table.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the brief and the controller rulings, the old signatures are dropped and re-granted cleanly, and the new function bodies change only what they should. The remaining items are low-severity edge cases (the replay near the 7-day boundary, notice hiding by backdating) and small test gaps.

### Findings
1. ADDRESSED. The wrapper now does the replay lookup first, at `supabase/migrations/20261030130002_device_occurred_at.sql:62-72`. If the caller is the child, it looks up the key `purchase:<child>:<key>` and returns the original entry. Otherwise it falls through to `record_purchase_at(..., resolve_occurred_at(p))` at :74-76. The insert logic is not duplicated, and a new key still gets the time check (22023) and the ledger advisory lock. The two required pgTAP cases are in `supabase/tests/database/purchases.test.sql`, added after the `d1` replay cases and before the `resolve_occurred_at is not granted` check (the diff shows no line numbers for them). They are `a replay with an out-of-range time still returns the original entry` plus a one-row-count check, and `a new key with an 8-day-old time still raises 22023`. The plan went from 73 to 76, which matches the three new assertions. I did not re-run the suites.
2. ADDRESSED. `supabase/tests/database/learning_progress.test.sql` gains two cases: `a completion stamped before the attempt started is accepted`, and `a completion stamped before started_at stores started_at`, which asserts `completed_at = started_at`. The plan went from 92 to 94. The test starts a fresh mission, so `started_at` is now and the completion is stamped 1 hour back, which does exercise the clamp.
3. ADDRESSED. `task-4-report.md:32` now describes the real order: the caller and key lookup first, then `resolve_occurred_at`, then `record_purchase_at`, which takes the ledger lock and re-checks the key. Lines 97 and 104 are consistent with it. Line 38 still says "plan 65 → 73". That is stale for the round-1 plan numbers and is cosmetic.

Specific checks:
- (a) Reply shape is identical. The original replay inside `record_purchase_at` (`20261029183422_purchases.sql:172-175`) returns `{status:'recorded', entry_id, notices:0}`, and the early return is the same literal. The original never reported real notices on a replay either, so nothing new can differ for any caller. `status` and `entry_id` are present, and `free_minor` only appears on `not_enough_spend`, which a replay never returns.
- (b) No leak. The gate is `is_self(p_child_id)` plus an approved child membership, which is the same gate as in `record_purchase_at`. The key embeds `p_child_id`, so the lookup can only match that child's own entries. A non-self caller falls through and gets JA403 from `record_purchase_at` as before.
- (c) The key is a purchase key. The only other ledger key written in this file is `'reversal:' || id` (`purchases.sql:282`), and I found no other use of the `purchase:` prefix in the migrations. A collision is not possible.
- (d) A purchase that was later undone still has its original entry, because the reversal is a separate row with a `reversal:` key. A replay therefore returns the original `recorded` and `entry_id`, the same as before the fix, and now also when the time has gone out of range.
- (e) The wrapper is `security definer` with `set search_path = ''`. The body uses schema-qualified `public.*` and `pg_catalog.jsonb_build_object`. The revoke from public/anon and the grant to authenticated at :84-85 are unchanged, and the `comment on` text was updated.
- Race: the early lookup runs without the lock. If a concurrent first insert is not yet visible, the call falls through to `record_purchase_at`, which takes the lock and re-checks the key, so the double-debit protection is intact. A null or blank key makes the concatenation null or non-matching and falls through to the existing validation.

### New Breakage in Fix Diff
None.

### Out-of-Scope Observations
- For a new key, a bad `p_occurred_at` still returns 22023 before JA403 and the validation errors. This is the already-accepted minor.
- The early path skips the amount and label validation for a replay, so a replay with an invalid amount returns the original entry rather than 22023. This matches the old behaviour, where the replay also came after validation. The validation is not skipped when no entry exists.

### Verdict
All findings addressed: yes

### Spec Compliance
- `offlineCopy` (`copy.ts:2-8`): all five keys. Offline, slow and updated strings match the constraints verbatim. OK.
- `useOnline`, `reportNetworkFailure`, `reportNetworkSuccess` (`use-online.ts`): `useSyncExternalStore` over a module store seeded from `navigator.onLine`, updated by window events and the report functions, server snapshot `true`. OK.
- `runOnline` (`online-action.ts:484-509`): offline guard returns before calling the action; rejection maps to the offline copy; 15 000 ms default timeout maps to the slow copy; "Failed to find Server Action" maps to the updated copy; the timed-out action is left to settle. OK.
- `useOnlineActionState` (`:512-519`): same tuple as `useActionState`, with `runOnline` applied inside. OK.
- `OfflineBanner`: `role="status"`, `bg-muted`, `text-ink-2`, no red, renders nothing when online. Mounted under the header in the `(app)` layout. OK.
- (e) A timeout does not mark the connection offline (`:498` calls `reportNetworkSuccess` only when the result is not the slow message; only a rejection calls `reportNetworkFailure`). Consistent with the brief ("timeout -> slow copy", "rejection -> offline"). OK.
- (f) `error.tsx`: heading and body match the brief, the button is unchanged, and the hunk context shows the `useEffect` import intact. The `"use client"` line is above the hunk and untouched, so it stays a client component. OK.
- `experimental.useOffline` is not enabled, `index.ts` exports nothing server-only, no `console.log`, and every hook-using file has `"use client"`. OK.
- All six required tests exist under the exact names the brief gives.
- Unverified: I did not run `pnpm check`, which the brief expects green.

Named-risk checks (from the diff only):
- (a) Module-load listeners: guarded by `typeof window !== "undefined"`, so SSR is safe, with one registration per module instance. Under HMR a re-evaluated module leaves the old instance's listeners behind; they only mutate the stale module's state, so the effect is a small dev-only leak, not a bug.
- (b) Timer and late rejection: `clearTimeout` runs in `finally`, so no zombie timer. `Promise.race` attaches handlers to `action()`, so a rejection after the timeout is handled, not unhandled. OK.
- (c) `useActionState` semantics: pending flag and form-action identity come from `useActionState` unchanged; the inline closure is fine. Typing is `FormData` payload with `ActionState<T>` as prior state and `null` as initial state. Unverified: `null` as the initial state is correct only if `ActionState` includes `null` (`idle === null`). It compiles, so I assume so.
- (d) Hydration: the server snapshot `true` is used during hydration and the client snapshot applied afterwards, so no mismatch error. OK.

### Strengths
- Small and tight, with clear separation: copy, store, wrapper and UI in their own files.
- Cleanup is correct: timer cleared in `finally`, subscribe/unsubscribe balanced.
- Tests check behaviour: the action is never called when offline, the fake-timer 15 s case, and `isOnline()` stays `true` after a timeout or an update error.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **Offline state can get stuck (plan-mandated design).** `reportNetworkFailure()` sets the store offline, and `runOnline` then refuses to call any action while offline (`online-action.ts:488`).
   - Recovery happens only on a browser `online` event or a later `reportNetworkSuccess()`.
   - After a rejection while `navigator.onLine` stays true, no event fires and `runOnline` never calls the action again, so `reportNetworkSuccess()` is unreachable. Examples: a transient fetch failure, a captive portal, or a server-side throw that surfaces as a rejection.
   - The banner then shows permanently and every online-only action is blocked until a reload.
   - Suggested fix: add a recovery path, either an optional re-probe or letting the next attempt through after a short cooldown. Task 6's outbox may already cover this; confirm it does.
   - The `catch` treats every rejection as a network failure, including unexpected server errors, which widens the stuck-offline case.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. Fragile timeout detection: `online-action.ts:498` identifies a timeout by comparing the result message to `offlineCopy.slow`. A sentinel or flag would be sturdier.
2. No test for the cleanup paths in (b): nothing covers the cleared timer or a late rejection after a timeout.
3. Live-region announcement: `role="status"` is mounted together with its text. Some screen readers announce a live region only when its content changes after mount, so the banner may not be read out.
4. HMR listener leak (check (a)), dev-only.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** The implementation matches the brief, the exact names, copy and styling, and risks (b), (c), (d) and (f) check out. The one real concern is that a sticky offline state has no recovery path, which comes from the plan's design; it needs a follow-up in Task 6 or Task 10, not a block here.

## Task Description
Read your task brief first: /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-30-network-resilience/task-6-brief.md — your requirements, exact values verbatim.
Binding project-wide constraints: /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-30-network-resilience/global-constraints.md (outbox expiry 7 days / 20 attempts; backoff 2 s, 5 s, 15 s, 30 s, then 60 s cap; outbox send timeout 15 000 ms; exact copy; Review Focus items 1, 3, 4, 5 are owned by this task's tests).

## Context
Part of a network-resilience plan for Jarso (Next.js 16 + Supabase pocket-money app). This is the on-device outbox that stores a child's own actions (diary purchases, mission steps, practice, check-ins) in IndexedDB when the network fails and sends them one at a time, oldest first, when it returns. Money safety depends on it: an item must be stored before the first send, its idempotency key never regenerated, two tabs must never send the same item concurrently, and another user's items must never be sent or shown.

Already built (Task 5), client-only module `src/features/offline/` with barrel `index.ts`: `offlineCopy` (copy.ts: offline, slow, waiting, expired, updated), `useOnline()`, `reportNetworkFailure()`, `reportNetworkSuccess()` (use-online.ts), `runOnline`, `useOnlineActionState` (online-action.ts), `OfflineBanner`. Add your exports to that barrel. Task 7 (next) builds `POST /api/v1/outbox`; Task 8/9 consume `submit`, `useOutbox`, `remove`, `OutboxSync`.

Controller rulings (binding, they amend the brief):
- R1: `sendToServer` posts `{ kind, payload: { ...item.payload, occurredAt: item.occurredAt } }` to `/api/v1/outbox`; the item keeps `occurredAt` at top level for expiry. The server parses `payload` with each feature's own Zod schema (which has optional `occurredAt`).
- R2: the offline module must NOT import from `@/features/diary` (that would create a barrel cycle — diary will import offline in Task 8). So IGNORE the brief's instruction to move the not_enough_spend sentence into `src/features/diary/copy.ts` and special-case a 200 `not_enough_spend` in `sendToServer`. Instead `sendToServer` stays generic: 200 → `done` with `data`; network/timeout/5xx → `retry`; 401 → `unauthorized`; any other 4xx → `refused` with the JSON body's `error.message` (response shape: `{ error: { code, message } }` from `src/lib/api/index.ts`). Task 7's route will turn a not_enough_spend purchase into a 409 with the right sentence.
- Task 1 made the server return 503 `{code:"JA503"}`-style errors as `UNAVAILABLE` → HTTP 503 via withApi; that's a `retry`.
- `submit`'s immediate send: when the browser says offline (`navigator.onLine === false`), skip the immediate send and return `queued` (item already stored).
- `idb` and `fake-indexeddb` are new dependencies (`pnpm add idb`, `pnpm add -D fake-indexeddb`). Check the exact current versions you install and note them in the report.

Read CLAUDE.md and docs/conventions.md first (client-only module: "use client" where hooks/components are used; no server-only imports; no console.log; logger from @/lib is fine). NEVER read .env* files. Logs must not carry payloads, labels or keys.

Work directly on branch `main` in /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso (user's explicit choice). Conventional Commits, subject ≤60 chars, body lines ≤100, NO AI attribution. `pnpm check` green before committing (known flaky, unrelated: rule-row.test.tsx; two pre-existing lint warnings in learning/server/service.test.ts are expected).
