This is money-safety code. Named risks worth one check each:
(a) Store-before-send: an item is durably written before the first network attempt; a crash mid-send leaves it `waiting` or `sending` — and a `sending` item left by a crashed tab is recovered (not stuck forever as `sending`).
(b) Idempotency key never regenerated across retries, reloads, or "Change it" flows (only a new submit gets a new key).
(c) Web Lock: flush is serialised per user across tabs; `ifAvailable: true` no-op path doesn't drop a needed flush permanently (e.g. a later trigger retries); fallback when navigator.locks is undefined. Also: does `submit`'s immediate send take the same lock, or can it race a concurrent flush in another tab sending the same item?
(d) Per-user isolation: listForUser filters by userId; flush and useOutbox never touch another user's items; an item can't be sent under a different signed-in session.
(e) Order: oldest-first, stop at first retry/unauthorized, and nextAttemptAt respected; `submit` sending older waiting items first doesn't bypass backoff or the lock.
(f) Timers/listeners: OutboxSync clears its 30 s interval and removes online/visibilitychange/onNetworkSuccess listeners on unmount and on userId change (no zombie timers, no double flush loops).
(g) IndexedDB unavailable → submit returns `unsaved`, never throws, never silently drops.
(h) State machine: exact backoff values, 20th failure → needs_look with expired copy, older than 7 days → needs_look, unauthorized unchanged, done deletes.

### Spec Compliance

- Types (`OutboxKind`, `OutboxItem`, `SendOutcome`, `SubmitResult`) match the brief. `types.ts` is correct.
- `nextState` is correct (state.ts:11-34). Backoff index `BACKOFF_MS[attempts-1]` gives 2 s, 5 s, 15 s, 30 s, then 60 s. The 20th failure becomes needs_look with `offlineCopy.expired`. Age over 7 days is measured from `occurredAt`. unauthorized returns the same object. done returns null. Risk (h) passes.
- Store matches the brief: `jarso-outbox`/`items`/index `userId`. `open()` returns null when `indexedDB` is missing or the open throws, and does not cache a failure (store.ts:66-96). Risk (g) passes for the open path.
- R1 body is followed (send.ts:9-12) and tested (send.test.ts:35-55).
- R2 is followed: no `@/features/diary` import, and the send mapping is generic.
- **R13 is violated.** send.ts:46-51 sends every non-401 4xx to `refused`, so 408 and 429 become needs_look instead of `retry`. Neither status is tested.
- The `navigator.onLine === false` skip is in place (submit.ts:47) and tested.
- (a) Store-before-send passes. `put` comes before `drainOutbox` (submit.ts:41-49), and the test checks the stored row while fetch hangs. `"sending"` is never written, so a crashed tab can't leave an item stuck in that state. Web Locks are released when a tab dies.
- (b) Key reuse passes inside this module. The purchase `id` is the key, retries re-send the stored item, and nothing regenerates the key. ⚠️ Whether "Change it" reuses or replaces the key is the caller's job (Tasks 8/9), and can't be checked here.
- (c) Lock passes. `submit` goes through the same `drainOutbox` → `exclusive` → `navigator.locks.request(..., {ifAvailable:true})` as `flush` (flush.ts:18-30), so it can't race another tab. A cross-tab BUSY is not remembered (`again` is in-tab only). The next trigger picks the item up: the 30 s interval (the BroadcastChannel makes `waiting` true in this tab), `online` or `visibilitychange`. So nothing is dropped permanently, but it can wait up to 30 s. When `navigator.locks` is missing it runs unlocked with the in-tab guard, which is tested.
- (d) Per-user isolation passes on the client. `getAllFromIndex(userId)` and `flush(userId)` are both scoped. The cross-session case fails; see Important #2.
- (e) Order passes. Items are sorted by createdAt then id. needs_look items are skipped. The drain breaks at the first item that isn't due yet and at the first retry or unauthorized (flush.ts:37-50). `submit` drains older items under the same lock, so it doesn't skip backoff.
- (f) Timers and listeners pass. Both effects return cleanups keyed on `userId` (outbox-sync.tsx:15-35). Re-entry, such as `onNetworkSuccess` firing in the middle of a drain, is absorbed by `running`/`again`. The unmount test covers online and network success. Two things are untested: the 30 s interval and switching userId.
- The brief's 11 named tests are all present.

### Strengths
- `nextState` is small, pure and well tested, including the 19-vs-20 and just-under-7-days boundary controls.
- The two-tab test uses `vi.resetModules()` so the second "tab" shares only the database and the lock. The implementer reports a mutation check (Web Lock removed, in-tab guard kept) that fails this test. I didn't re-run it.
- `submit` drains older items under the shared lock instead of sending its own item out of band, which keeps step → completeMission order.
- Logs carry only kind, status and reason: no payloads, keys or messages.
- Fixture times are relative to the clock, so the suite won't start failing 7 days from now.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **408 and 429 are treated as refused (R13).** At send.ts:46, add `if (response.status === 408 || response.status === 429)` → `retry` before the refusal branch, and add a test for each. As it stands, a rate limit turns a child's purchase into "Needs a look" with server text.
2. **An item can be sent under a different child's session.** sendToServer posts no user identity (send.ts:9-12), so the server can't tell when `item.userId` differs from the session. Scenario on a family device: child A's tab is still open with `<OutboxSync userId=A>`, and child B signs in from another tab. Sign-out is a server action (`src/features/auth/server/actions.ts:55`), not a hard reload. On the next visibilitychange, online event or 30 s tick, A's queued purchases POST with B's cookies and could be recorded against B's jar. This is Review Focus 4 in the cross-session direction; the client-only test doesn't cover it. Fix: send `userId` in the body, or as a header, and have the Task 7 route answer 401 on a mismatch, which leaves the item unchanged. That changes the binding R1 body shape, so the controller needs to rule on it.
3. **`submit` and `flush` can throw after the item is stored**, which breaks "never throws". `drain` doesn't catch `store.listForUser`, `store.put` or `store.remove` (flush.ts:35, 47-48), for example after `blocking()` closes the db. `crypto.randomUUID()` (submit.ts:31) is undefined outside secure contexts, such as LAN http testing on a phone. In `submit` an exception reaches the form, even though the item may already be saved or sent. In `OutboxSync` (`void flush(...)`) it becomes an unhandled rejection. Fix: wrap the post-put drain in submit with try/catch → `queued`, and catch inside `drainOutbox`.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
- **The stored `idempotencyKey` is never sent for non-purchase kinds** (implementer concern 1). Server idempotency depends on callers duplicating the key into `payload`. Either merge it into the body or state that contract in `types.ts`.
- **`submit` during an in-tab drain returns `queued`**, for example right after mount while the initial flush runs. The `again` rerun then sends the item moments later. The form briefly shows "Waiting to send" while online.
- **Early backoff steps run late.** Only the 30 s interval and events trigger a flush, so the 2 s and 5 s steps actually wait up to 30 s. Nothing schedules a flush at `nextAttemptAt`. This is within the brief, but the brief's backoff numbers mostly have no effect.
- **`FALLBACK_REFUSAL` is an inline copy** of the 22023 wording (send.ts:7). It belongs in `copy.ts`.
- **Server refusal text is shown raw.** `withApi`'s "Invalid request" would reach a child (concern 4). Task 7 needs to map refusals to child-safe text.
- **`submit` with an existing purchase key overwrites the stored row** (`put`), resetting attempts and status. Fine if "Change it" is meant to work that way; confirm in Task 9.
- **A 2xx with no JSON body** (for example 204) is retried until "waited too long", even though the server committed it. The route must always return a JSON body.
- **Barrel names are generic.** Exports `remove`, `flush` and `submit` from `@/features/offline` are likely to collide. Consider `removeOutboxItem` and similar.
- **`test-utils.ts` is a non-test module** inside the feature folder. Harmless, since only tests import it.
- **Test gaps:** no tests for the 30 s interval, a userId change, a timeout or a non-JSON 200.
- **Noise in the report:** the "known-flaky" `rule-row.test.tsx` failure and the 2 pre-existing lint warnings were explained away rather than tracked.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** The state machine, store-before-send, locking, ordering and cleanup are solid and well tested. The R13 mapping still needs fixing, and the post-store throw paths need catching. The cross-session send (stale tab, new child signed in) needs a controller ruling before Task 7 builds the route.

### Findings
1. ADDRESSED. `send.ts` (diff lines 777-781) now maps 408 and 429 to `retry`, placed before the 5xx and refusal branches. Tests `a 408 is a retry` and `a 429 is a retry, not a refusal` are in `send.test.ts`. The branch logs a warn with kind and status only, and does not call `reportNetworkFailure`, which is right for a 429.
2. ADDRESSED. The body is `{ kind, userId: item.userId, payload: { ...payload, occurredAt } }` (`send.ts` `body()`), exactly per R14. The `send.test.ts` shape assertion now includes `userId: item.userId`. `types.ts` documents that idempotency material must be inside `payload` and that only `kind`, `userId` and `payload` go over the wire.
3. ADDRESSED.
   - `submit.ts`: the post-put drain is in a try/catch that returns `queued`. The test `returns queued when the drain throws after storing` also checks the item is still stored.
   - `flush.ts`: `drainOutbox` wraps `drainOnce` in a try/catch that logs `logger.warn("Outbox flush stopped", { userId, reason: <error name> })` and returns null. There is no payload, key or message in the log. `flushOutbox` therefore never rejects. The test `swallows a store error and logs` covers this.
   - `uuid.ts` provides the `crypto.randomUUID` fallback and is used for non-purchase ids in `submit`.
4. ADDRESSED. The barrel exports `flushOutbox`, `removeOutboxItem` and `submitToOutbox`, and keeps `useOutbox` and `OutboxSync` (`index.ts`). The renames are applied in `flush.ts`, `store.ts`, `submit.ts` and the tests. A grep of `src` for importers of the old names `flush`, `submit` or `remove` from the offline module found none.
5. ADDRESSED. `outbox-sync.tsx` has a third effect. It takes the minimum strictly-future `nextAttemptAt` over `waiting` items, sets one `setTimeout`, and clears it in the effect cleanup. The effect depends on `[userId, items]`. Both named tests are present. The unmount test asserts `vi.getTimerCount() === 0`, which also covers the 30 s interval.
6. ADDRESSED. `offlineCopy.refused` was added to `copy.ts` and `send.ts` uses it. The new test asserts the fallback.

Specific checks:
- (a) No tight loop.
  - Only items with `nextAttemptAt > now` get a timer. An item that is due or in the past gets none.
  - The effect re-runs only when `useOutbox` emits a new array, and that happens only after a store `put`/`remove`, locally or via cross-tab notify. The snapshot is cached and `EMPTY` is stable.
  - A flush that is BUSY in another tab, or that gets `unauthorized`, writes nothing. The items are unchanged, no timer is re-armed, and the 30 s poll is the fallback.
  - A flush that gets `retry` writes a `nextAttemptAt` at least 2 s ahead. That is the intended backoff, capped at 20 attempts.
  - Timer delays are at most 60 s, so there is no setTimeout overflow.
- (b) The catch does not swallow a state transition.
  - `unauthorized` returns the same object, so there is no write and nothing is lost.
  - `refused` does a single `put`. If that `put` throws, the item stays `waiting` untouched. It is resent later, and the idempotency key turns that into a server replay. Each item is updated by one atomic IndexedDB operation, so nothing is half-updated.
  - `running` is cleaned up in the `finally` of `exclusive`. A throw does not leave a stuck in-tab guard.
- (c) The uuid fallback is correct. `bytes[6]` is `(x & 0x0f) | 0x40` and `bytes[8]` is `(x & 0x3f) | 0x80`. The output uses the 8-4-4-4-12 layout, and the test regex `4xxx-[89ab]xxx` passes. It satisfies `z.uuid()`.
- (d) The `copy.ts` change only appends the `refused` key. The existing strings are untouched. The wording is identical to the old inline `FALLBACK_REFUSAL`. The `export * from "./copy"` barrel passes the new key through.

### New Breakage in Fix Diff
None Critical or Important. Minor items:
- The timer can fire a few ms before `nextAttemptAt`, for example with clock granularity or skew. `drain` then breaks on "not yet due" and writes nothing. No timer is re-armed because `items` is unchanged, so the retry waits for the next 30 s poll, online or visibility event. It is bounded and self-healing, not a loop.
- If a later item's `put` or `remove` throws in the same drain, `submit` returns `queued` even when its own item was already sent and removed. The item is gone and nothing is duplicated, but the form shows the "waiting" state momentarily. Rare.
- The `flush.test.ts` title `a flushOutbox asked for mid-flushOutbox in this tab runs once more afterwards` is a find-and-replace artifact. It is cosmetic.

### Out-of-Scope Observations
- The `uuid()` fallback only protects ids generated inside `submit`. Callers that mint `idempotencyKey` with `crypto.randomUUID()` (the Task 8/9 forms) would still throw in an insecure context. They should use the same helper, which is not exported from the barrel.
- 429 retries ignore any `Retry-After` header and count toward the 20-attempt cap. This is acceptable for about 60 users.
- The `.claude-logs` file is modified in the commit, as the repo rules require.

### Verdict
All findings addressed: yes

## Context
Part of a network-resilience plan for Jarso (Next.js 16 + Supabase pocket-money app for children 11–14). A client-only device outbox (`src/features/offline/outbox/`, done in Task 6) sends a child's queued actions one at a time to `POST /api/v1/outbox`. You build that route. It must reuse the existing feature services (same Zod schemas, same `assertRole("child")` checks) — no second validation or business path.

What the client sends and how it reads the response (fixed by Task 6, `src/features/offline/outbox/send.ts`):
- Body: `{ kind, userId, payload: { ...item.payload, occurredAt } }`. Kinds: "purchase" | "practice" | "step" | "completeMission" | "checkIn".
- 200 with JSON `{ data }` → done. 401 → unauthorized (item paused unchanged). 408/429/5xx/network → retry. Any other 4xx → refused, showing the body's `error.message` to the CHILD.

Controller rulings (binding, amend the brief):
- R14: the route must answer 401 UNAUTHORIZED when `body.userId` ≠ the signed-in user's id (a stale tab of child A must not post into child B's jar). Check this after authenticating, before calling any service.
- R2: a purchase whose service result is `{ status: "not_enough_spend", freeMinor }` must become a refusal: throw `AppError("CONFLICT", notEnoughSpendMessage(freeMinor, currency))`. Create `src/features/diary/copy.ts` exporting `notEnoughSpendMessage(freeMinor: number, currency: string): string` with exactly the sentences now inline in `src/features/diary/components/purchase-sheet.tsx` (the empty-jar sentence when freeMinor <= 0, otherwise the "That's more than you have in Spend (…)" sentence using `formatMoney(..., { showMinor: "nonzero" })`), export it from the diary barrel, and switch purchase-sheet.tsx to use it (no behaviour change there).
- Child-safe refusals: a child sees `error.message`. `withApi`'s Zod mapping returns "Invalid request"; for this route, parse with `safeParse` (or catch ZodError) and throw `AppError("VALIDATION", "Please check what you entered and try again.")` instead — reuse the existing 22023 wording from `src/lib/db-error.ts` rather than a new string. Unknown `kind` is also a VALIDATION with that message.
- The route must always return a JSON body on 2xx (the client retries a 2xx without JSON).
- Task 4 added optional `occurredAt` to `purchaseSchema`, `recordPracticeSchema`, `completeMissionSchema`; the step and check-in schemas have none — strip/ignore it for those kinds rather than failing (decide via each schema's behaviour with unknown keys; say what you did).
- `purchaseSchema(currency)` needs the family currency: get it from `getFamilyContext()` in `@/features/allowance/server`, as `src/features/diary/server/actions.ts` does.
- Follow the api-endpoint skill (.claude/skills/api-endpoint/SKILL.md) and `src/app/api/v1/me/route.ts` as the pattern; `withApi` + `json`; `export const maxDuration = 30`. Document the endpoint in docs/api.md.

(e) Exporting recordStep/recordPractice/completeMission/completeCheckIn from the learning server barrel doesn't leak server code into a client barrel.

### Spec Compliance
- **Route and runtime: compliant.** `POST = withApi(...)` and `maxDuration = 30` are at src/app/api/v1/outbox/route.ts:15,45.
- **R14: compliant.** `assertUser()` runs first (route.ts:46), and the `userId !== user.id` check throws 401 before `getFamilyContext` or any service call (route.ts:51-52). The test asserts that neither is called (route.test.ts:108-113).
- **R2: compliant.** `not_enough_spend` becomes `AppError("CONFLICT", notEnoughSpendMessage(freeMinor, currency))` (route.ts:24-26). src/features/diary/copy.ts holds the two sentences, copied verbatim, and is exported from the diary barrel (diary/index.ts:+4).
- **Child-safe refusals: compliant.** Bad JSON, an invalid payload and an unknown kind all give `AppError("VALIDATION", "Please check what you entered and try again.")` (route.ts:18,47-49,55). I checked this string against `src/lib/db-error.ts:15` (22023) and it is identical. withApi's "Invalid request" can't be reached because the route uses `safeParse`.
- **Discriminated union: compliant.** It is built from the features' own schemas (offline/schemas.ts:15-24), and money goes through `purchaseSchema(currency)` to `amountMinor` (test :60-65).
- **2xx always JSON: compliant.** Every success goes through `json(...)`, so the body is always `{ data }`.
- **docs/api.md: compliant.** The table row and a status-to-outbox-behaviour table are added.
- **Brief's five tests plus the controller's three: all present** (route.test.ts).
- **Rule 9 (Route Handlers via `parseBody`): deliberate deviation, accepted.** The ruling requires child-safe wording, and `parseBody` would throw a ZodError that becomes "Invalid request". Zod still validates every input.
- **Nothing extra.** The learning server barrel exports and the offline barrel `schemas` export are both needed by the Interfaces section.

Named-risk checks (one each):
- **(a) Security: OK.**
  - I read `src/lib/api`. withApi calls `assertSameOrigin` before the handler, and the test confirms `assertUser` is not called on a cross-origin request.
  - Services take the child from the session, never from the body: `recordPurchase` uses `assertRole("child")` and `childId: user.id` (diary/server/service.ts:17-19), and learning uses `assertChild()` plus `ownAttempt(..., user.id)`.
  - So `body.userId` is only compared against the session, never used to write. A signed-in parent gets 401 (id mismatch) or 403 (assertRole). An anonymous user gets 401 from `assertUser`.
- **(b) Idempotency: OK.** `purchaseSchema` passes `idempotencyKey` and `occurredAt` through (test :60-65), and the service forwards both to the repository (service.ts:22-23). `completeMission(attemptId, occurredAt)` and `recordPractice` keep `occurredAt` (learning/schemas.ts:300-310).
- **(c) Error mapping: OK for this task.**
  - UNAVAILABLE maps to 503 (errors.ts:9), and the test covers it.
  - INTERNAL texts such as "Failed to record the purchase" (diary/server/repository.ts:28,36) come back as 500. The client retries on 5xx, so the child never sees them as a refusal. The online form already shows the same text, so this is not new.
- **(d) purchase-sheet refactor: behaviour is identical.** Same `freeMinor <= 0` branch, same strings, same `{ showMinor: "nonzero" }`. Only the unused `formatMoney` import was dropped.
- **(e) Learning barrel: no leak.** `src/features/learning/server/index.ts` starts with `import "server-only"`, and the four functions were added only there.
- **(f) Missing userId → 401: acceptable.** It fails closed, and the Task 6 client always sends `userId`, so only a hand-crafted request gets there. The item pauses rather than being refused, which is the safer of the two.

### Strengths
- The order is right: auth, then the R14 check before any database read, then validation, then the service. A stale tab can't trigger any database work.
- There is only one validation path, the features' own schemas, so the offline and online paths can't drift apart.
- Refusals never carry Zod details, which is the right call for children, and the docs say why.
- The tests mock at the server-barrel boundary and check results the client sees: status codes, exact messages, and what reached each service. The check-in test proves the extra `occurredAt` is stripped.
- The R2 copy is now shared and has its own test (copy.test.ts).

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **Copied string.** route.ts:18 repeats the 22023 sentence from src/lib/db-error.ts:15 instead of sharing one constant, so the two could drift. Exporting a named constant from `@/lib` would fix it.
2. **Server route loads client code.** route.ts imports the client-side `@/features/offline` barrel just for the schema. Along with it come `outbox/store.ts` (which imports `idb`) and the client hooks. I grepped those modules: there are no top-level side effects, and the report says the build passed, so it works. It is still loose coupling. Moving `outboxRequestSchema` to a small module that doesn't pull in IndexedDB code would keep the route's imports small.
3. **Routing tests incomplete.** No test covers the `practice`, `step` or `completeMission` branches. In particular, nothing checks that `completeMission` receives `occurredAt` (route.ts:34-35). The code is short and correct by inspection, but one `completeMission` test would protect the most important pass-through.
4. **Retry loop on 500 (Task 6 contract, outside this task).** A deterministic INTERNAL error, such as the RPC returning an unexpected result (diary/server/repository.ts:36), gives 500 and the client retries forever with backoff. It is worth having an attempt cap, or treating INTERNAL as "needs a look", on the client side.
5. **Cross-origin 403 wording.** The text "Cross-origin request rejected" is not child-worded. The report notes that same-origin app fetches can't hit it, so this is harmless.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** All requirements and binding rulings are implemented and tested. The security risks I checked hold: CSRF guard, session-only child identity, R14 before any service call, and the idempotency key and `occurredAt` passed through. What remains is small cleanup: a copied string, the client barrel import, and missing tests for three of the routing branches.

## Task Description

## Context
Part of a network-resilience plan for Jarso (Next.js 16 + Supabase pocket-money app for children 11–14). This is where the child sees it: "Spent something?" saves the purchase on the device when offline and sends it later; queued items show inline as "Waiting to send" or "Needs a look".

Built already (use these exact names; the brief's older names are superseded):
- `@/features/offline` (client barrel): `submitToOutbox(input: { userId; kind; payload; idempotencyKey; occurredAt? }) → Promise<{status:"sent";data} | {status:"queued"} | {status:"refused";message} | {status:"unsaved"}>`, `useOutbox(userId): OutboxItem[]`, `removeOutboxItem(...)` (check its exact signature in src/features/offline/outbox/store.ts / index.ts), `OutboxSync` (props `{ userId }`), `offlineCopy` (offline, slow, waiting, expired, updated, refused), `useOnline`, `runOnline`. `OutboxItem` has `status: "waiting" | "sending" | "needs_look"`, `message?`, `payload`, `kind`, `idempotencyKey`, `occurredAt`.
- `submitToOutbox` stores the item first, then (unless `navigator.onLine === false`) drains older items and this one under a Web Lock. A purchase item's `id` equals its idempotencyKey.
- `POST /api/v1/outbox` (src/app/api/v1/outbox/route.ts): body `{ kind, userId, payload }`; a purchase returns 200 `{ data: { status: "recorded", entryId, ... } }`; a not-enough-spend purchase is refused (409) with the sentence from `notEnoughSpendMessage(freeMinor, currency)` in new `src/features/diary/copy.ts` (exported from the diary barrel). So, contrary to the brief, `sent` + `not_enough_spend` no longer happens — the outcome is `refused` with that message. Ruling: on `refused` from the immediate send, show the message inline in the sheet, keep the sheet open and the fields, and remove the stored item (the child is right there to fix it).
- The payload for a purchase is exactly `purchaseSchema`'s INPUT: `{ amount: <the form's amount string>, label?, idempotencyKey }` — the route parses it with the same schema. Don't convert money on the client.
- Client-minted keys: use the outbox uuid helper (`src/features/offline/outbox/uuid.ts`, `uuid()`) — export it from the offline barrel (e.g. as `newId`) and use it instead of `crypto.randomUUID()` in purchase-sheet (randomUUID throws in insecure contexts). "Change it" must mint a NEW key and remove the old item only after the new submit returns sent/queued.
- After `sent` + recorded: the existing toast "Saved. Nice one.", close, and `router.refresh()` (import `useRouter` from "next/navigation"; Server Actions previously called `refresh()` server-side — this path doesn't go through an action).
- `recordPurchaseAction` (src/features/diary/server/actions.ts): grep for other callers; delete it if unused, per the brief.
- `userId` for the child page: the child page already resolves the signed-in child server-side behind Suspense — pass `user.id` down. For the layout, `ChildOutboxSync` is a small server component (in the child layout, behind `<Suspense fallback={null}>`) that reads the user via the auth DAL (`getCurrentUser` from `@/features/auth/server`; render nothing if signed out or not a child — use `currentRole` from `@/features/family/server`) and renders `<OutboxSync userId=... />`. Cache Components are on: anything reading the session must be inside Suspense; the layout itself stays static.
- e2e: Playwright config runs `pnpm build && pnpm start` (playwright.config.ts). Look at existing specs in tests/e2e (e.g. the purchase/diary and child-home specs) for the sign-in helpers and fixtures. For "lost response", use `page.route("**/api/v1/outbox", async (route) => { const r = await route.fetch(); await route.abort(); })` on the first call only. Use `context.setOffline(true/false)`.

Read CLAUDE.md, docs/design.md (voice + calm styling, keycap buttons, child-friendly copy), docs/testing.md, and the ui-component skill first. NEVER read .env* files. Copy for "Ask a parent" advice is fixed: "Show this to a parent and decide together."

- **Key minting: compliant.** It uses `newId`, once per open (purchase-sheet.tsx:1025). The barrel export is at offline/index.ts:1203-1204.
- **PendingPurchases: compliant.**
  - It shows this user's purchase items only (pending-purchases.tsx:648), and renders nothing when there are none (:661).
  - `waiting` and `sending` lines show the verbatim waiting copy (:739-742).
  - `needs_look` lines show the message plus Change it, Ask a parent and Remove (:718-737).
  - The advice sentence is verbatim (:629).
- **Change it: compliant.** It prefills from the stored payload (:634-640, :686), and the form mints a fresh key. The old item is removed in `onSaved`, which runs only on sent or queued (:687; purchase-sheet.tsx:1056, :1062).
- **Child page: compliant.** It passes `userId`, and the card sits above History (page.tsx:454-455).
- **Child layout: compliant.** The layout stays sync and static. `ChildOutboxSync` renders behind `<Suspense fallback={null}>` (layout.tsx:397-418).
- **`recordPurchaseAction`: deleted, as ruled.** It is removed from actions.ts and the barrel, and the undo-button mock is updated.
- **Jar balance: compliant.** Nothing subtracts pending items, and the e2e test asserts US$100 while the purchase waits offline (offline-purchase.spec.ts:1317).
- **Brief's 4 component tests and 3 e2e tests: all present**, plus extras.
- **Nothing missing or extra** beyond the rulings.
- ⚠️ I did not re-run the suites. The RED/GREEN results are the implementer's claims.

Named-risk checks (one each):
- **(a) Double debit: OK.**
  - The key is fixed per mounted form, and `submitToOutbox` stores a purchase under `id = idempotencyKey` (submit.ts, `id: input.kind === "purchase" ? input.idempotencyKey : uuid()`). A resend or a queued second action overwrites the same item, and the server replays.
  - Change it: the old item is `needs_look`, and `drainOutbox` skips anything that is not `waiting` (flush.ts:37). The old item can never be sent alongside the new one.
  - The old item is removed only after sent or queued. If that removal fails, the old item just stays a non-sending `needs_look`, so it still can't double-debit.
- **(b) Item actions: OK.** Remove calls `removeOutboxItem(item.id)` for that item only (:728). The store signature is `removeOutboxItem(id)` (store.ts:99). The prefill uses the stored strings. Ask a parent only sets local state. The unit tests cover all three.
- **(c) User scoping: OK.** `useOutbox` keeps its snapshots per user and uses `listForUser(userId)` (use-outbox.ts). The component filters to `kind === "purchase"` and hides when empty.
- **(d) Refresh loop: none.**
  - The refresh fires only when the count drops (:654-657).
  - `router.refresh()` keeps client state. A remount sets `previous` to the current count, so it can't fire again.
  - The double refresh after an immediate send (store, then remove, the count goes 0→1→0) is harmless. Remove on a `needs_look` item also triggers one unneeded refresh, which is also harmless.
- **(e) ChildOutboxSync: OK.**
  - `getCurrentUser` uses `getClaims()`, not `getSession` (auth/server/session.ts:20), and `currentRole` goes through the same DAL (family/server/dal.ts:71).
  - It returns null when signed out or not a child.
  - `unstable_rethrow` comes before the swallow, and the log carries only the error name.
- **(f) Lost-response e2e: OK.**
  - `route.fetch()` performs the real request before `route.abort()`, so the server commits on the first attempt.
  - The test asserts more than one call, Spend at US$50 (100 − 20 − 30, so a single debit), and one "Lost reply" entry after a reload.
- **(g) Copy: fits.** "Not sent yet" is plain, warm-coach wording (design.md §9). "Purchase" is plain and fine.
- **R17 cycle: confirmed.** offline/schemas.ts:2 imports `@/features/diary`, and the diary components import `@/features/offline`. Deferred per R17.

### Strengths
- One shared `PurchaseSheetContent` serves both "Spent something?" and Change it. The rule that the old item goes only after the new one is safe lives in a single `onSaved` hook.
- The amount display reuses `purchaseSchema` and `formatMoney`, so there is no second money parser.
- The tests check behaviour:
  - the old item is kept when the new one is refused;
  - the same key is reused after a refusal;
  - non-purchase kinds are ignored;
  - the background refresh happens.
- The e2e tests cover Review Focus 2 end to end and also check the unchanged balance while the purchase waits.
- The calm styling on the pending card is right: `text-ink-2`, muted surfaces, a status region for the advice, and an `aria-hidden` icon.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **Action buttons aren't tied to their item.** With two `needs_look` items, screen readers hear "Remove", "Change it" and "Ask a parent" twice with nothing to tell them apart (pending-purchases.tsx:722-730). The repo's own precedent is UndoButton's `aria-label={`Undo ${label}`}` (undo-button.tsx:31). Use, for example, `aria-label={`Remove ${label}`}`.
2. **Refusal styling doesn't match.** The sheet shows a refusal in `FormError`, which uses `text-destructive` and an alert icon (components/form/field.tsx:56). The same sentence shows calmly in `text-ink-2` on the pending card (pending-purchases.tsx:720). For not-enough-spend this red styling was already there, and the calm-styling rule is scoped to offline states, so it is not a violation. Still, `refused` now also covers the 22023 "check what you entered" case.
3. **Errors swallowed silently.** Remove swallows a failure (`.catch(() => {})`, :728), so the line just stays with no feedback. This is acceptable given the IndexedDB failure modes.
4. **The `sent` branch assumes `recorded`.** It doesn't check `data.status === "recorded"` (purchase-sheet.tsx:1055) and relies on the route turning `not_enough_spend` into 409. That is correct today but loosely coupled.
5. **No e2e for Change it.** It is covered only by unit tests.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** Every requirement and binding ruling is implemented. All seven named risks hold under focused checks: no double debit, user-scoped items, no refresh loop, the auth DAL used properly inside Suspense, and a real lost-response e2e. What remains is small accessibility and consistency polish.

## Task Description

## Context
Part of a network-resilience plan for Jarso (Next.js 16 + Supabase pocket-money app for children 11–14). The mission player (src/features/learning/components/mission-player.tsx) grades each step on the device for instant feedback and saves results through Server Actions; today a dropped connection shows "We couldn't save that. Check your connection and try again." (`call()` at :68-77). After this task, a started mission keeps going offline: step/practice/check-in/complete results go into the device outbox and send in order later.

Built already (use these exact names; the brief's older names are superseded):
- `@/features/offline` (client barrel): `submitToOutbox(input: { userId; kind; payload; idempotencyKey; occurredAt? })` → `{status:"sent";data} | {status:"queued"} | {status:"refused";message} | {status:"unsaved"}`; `useOutbox(userId)`; `runOnline(action, { timeoutMs? })` (refuses only when navigator.onLine === false; maps rejection/timeout to `{ ok:false, error:{ code:"UNAVAILABLE", message } }`); `offlineCopy` (offline, slow, waiting, expired, updated, refused); `newId()` for client ids (use instead of crypto.randomUUID).
- Kinds and their payloads (`POST /api/v1/outbox` parses `payload` with the feature's own Zod schema INPUT from `src/features/learning/schemas.ts`): "step" → recordStepSchema input; "practice" → recordPracticeSchema input; "completeMission" → completeMissionSchema input (`{ attemptId }`); "checkIn" → completeCheckInSchema input (`{ answers }`). `occurredAt` is added by the outbox for every kind (ignored where the schema lacks it). Server idempotency is natural: record_step_result PK (attempt, step, phase); practice unique per child/item/day; complete_mission returns false when already completed; complete_checkin on conflict do nothing. For these kinds pass `idempotencyKey: newId()` (it is only the local item id).
- The outbox sends oldest first, one at a time, under a Web Lock, so steps queued before completeMission arrive first.
- 200 response body is `{ data: <service result> }` — check what each learning service returns (src/features/learning/server/service.ts) and what the player currently does with each result (e.g. finish text after completeMission / completeCheckIn). On `queued`, build what the player shows from local state.
- `startMissionAction` stays an online Server Action: wrap the call in `runOnline`; when it fails with UNAVAILABLE show `offlineCopy.offline` and don't start.
- `userId`: the learning pages resolve the signed-in child server-side behind Suspense; pass `user.id` into `MissionPlayer` (find every page rendering it: `grep -rl "MissionPlayer" src/app`).
- `OutboxSync` is already mounted in the child layout (`ChildOutboxSync`), so queued items flush on reconnect without anything else on the page.
- "Saved on this device" note: small, calm (`text-ink-2`), under the progress bar, shown while this user has any waiting/sending item (`useOutbox(userId)`); a needs_look learning item may just show the existing gentle failure text — don't build a learning needs-look UI.

Read CLAUDE.md, docs/design.md (voice + calm styling), docs/testing.md first. NEVER read .env* files. e2e: Playwright runs `pnpm build && pnpm start`; reuse helpers in tests/e2e (see tests/e2e/learning*.spec.ts and tests/e2e/offline-purchase.spec.ts for the setOffline pattern).

Work directly on branch `main` in /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso (user's explicit choice). Conventional Commits, subject ≤60 chars, body lines ≤100, NO AI attribution. `pnpm check` green and the e2e spec passing before committing (known flaky, unrelated: rule-row.test.tsx; two pre-existing lint warnings expected).

### Spec Compliance
- `MissionPlayer` gains `userId: string`, and all four pages pass `user.id` from `requireRole("child", …)` inside their existing `<Suspense>` (mission :21/:70, check-in :14/:58, practice :19/:71, topic :25/:81). Verified, so (e) holds.
- Step, practice, check-in and complete calls go through `submitToOutbox` with `idempotencyKey: newId()`, and each payload is the schema input (`save()` at mission-player.tsx diff ~:987-999; step/practice ~:1190-1207; checkIn ~:1100; completeMission ~:1129). Matches the controller's names and kinds.
- `queued` lets the player go on with local grading. `refused` uses the red alert path. `unsaved` shows `offlineCopy.offline` in calm styling (`ProblemText`, `role="status"`, `bg-muted text-ink-2`), and the step stays so Continue retries. Matches.
- "Saved on this device." sits under the progress bar and shows only for `waiting`/`sending` items from `useOutbox(userId)`. Matches. The deviation of also showing it on the finish screen is reasonable.
- (a) Order holds. I checked `src/features/offline/outbox/flush.ts`: `drain()` walks `listForUser` (sorted by `createdAt`, which `createdNow()` keeps strictly increasing within a tab), skips non-waiting items, and `break`s when the oldest waiting item is still backing off. So when an earlier step was queued after a failed send and `completeMission` is then submitted online, `submitToOutbox` → `drainOutbox` sends the older step first. If that step retries, the drain stops and `completeMission` comes back `queued`. It can never overtake. One caveat: a `needs_look` step is skipped (`continue`), so the completion behind it is sent and refused. That is the implementer's Concern 3. It is recoverable and becomes deletion under R18.
- (b) Clean. I grepped the repo (excluding node_modules, .next, logs and sdd): there are no remaining references to `recordStepAction`, `recordPracticeAction`, `completeMissionAction` or `completeCheckInAction`. `startMissionAction` is used only by the player and its test. `learning/server/index.ts` still exports `completeCheckIn`, `completeMission`, `recordPractice` and `recordStep`, which `src/app/api/v1/outbox/route.ts` uses. `docs/api.md:59-61` already documents the outbox kinds and lists no learning Server Actions.
- (c) The change is legitimate synchronization, not a masked regression. `"sending"` is never written, so nothing can get stuck mid-send (grep: only `types.ts:21`). In-app navigation is a client-side transition, so the `put` and the send finish. Playwright's `goto` is a full document unload a few ms after the click, which can abort the IDB transaction or the fetch. If the item was stored, `ChildOutboxSync` sends it on the next page, but `/child` is server-rendered first, so the test raced. The worst real case, a hard unload within a few ms of tapping, costs one repeated step, and the server ignores duplicates by PK. The old Server Action fetch had the same exposure.
- (d) Correct. I checked `service.ts:179-188`: `completeMission` returns `attemptSummary(results)`, and `completeCheckIn` (:233-253) returns `{ correctCount }`. When sent, the player uses the server's data. When queued, `data` is `null` and it falls back to `attemptSummary(allResults)` / a local correct count (diff ~:1107-1109, ~:1135-1136). No crash. `allResults` includes the server results from resume.
- (f) Acceptable. `runOnline` returns `UNAVAILABLE` without calling the action when `navigator.onLine === false`. Its message for that case is exactly `offlineCopy.offline`, and the test asserts `startMissionAction` is not called. Showing `slow`/`updated` for the other cases is more accurate, and all of it is fixed calm copy (`src/features/offline/copy.ts`).
- (g) No duplicates. Check and Continue run inside `startSaving` with `loading={saving}`, which sets `disabled` (`src/components/ui/button.tsx:61`). Discrete clicks re-render synchronously, so a second tap hits a disabled button. Even if one got through, every kind is idempotent on the server.
- Tests: all three brief-named component tests are present, plus `unsaved`, `refused`, queued finish screen, queued check-in and practice payload. The e2e test with the brief's exact name is present and asserts order `step x6, completeMission` and that Learn shows the mission done.
- ⚠️ TDD evidence can't be verified, but it is coherent. Against HEAD's component, the new mock exposes only `startMissionAction`, so the old `recordStepAction` would be undefined → the `call()` catch → failures, and 11 failed / 2 passed fits. The e2e RED failure at spec :96 matches the line `getByText('Saved on this device.')`.

### Strengths
- `save()` turns the four-way `SubmitResult` into a small `Saved` union, so the four old copies of call-then-check collapse into one path. `Problem { text, calm }` keeps connection states out of red, and nothing in the copy says "error", "failed" or "invalid".
- The finish screen falls back to local state only when there is no server result, and the server's figures win when the item was sent.
- The e2e test checks the real order on the wire and the server's state afterwards, not just the UI.
- The second commit is a clean, focused removal of the dead actions, and the actions doc comment is updated.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. **"Saved on this device." flashes on every online save** (`src/features/learning/components/mission-player.tsx`, `SavedOnDevice`, diff ~:1310-1321, rendered at ~:1287 under the progress bar). `submitToOutbox` stores the item as `waiting` before sending, and `useOutbox` reacts to the store change. The note therefore appears for the length of every network request, even on a good connection, then disappears when the item is removed. That is a one-line layout shift above the step card on each Check/Continue. No test covers it: unit tests mock `useOutbox`, and the e2e test only checks the offline path. Fix: show the note only once an item has waited past a short grace period (for example `createdAt` older than about 1s, or a debounced visibility), or only after a send attempt has failed. Add a test for the online path showing no note.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. `saved.data as ReturnType<typeof attemptSummary> | null` and `as { correctCount: number } | null` (diff ~:1108, ~:1136) are unchecked casts of `unknown`. A small type guard or a shared response type would keep the player safe if a service's return shape changes.
2. The `tests/e2e/learning.spec.ts:1462-1463` (diff) comment explains the wait, but the old "click Continue and leave at once" case now has no coverage. If wanted, a test could assert that a step stored just before a hard reload is sent by `OutboxSync` on the next page.
3. `vi.restoreAllMocks()` in `afterEach` (test ~:601) only matters for the `navigator.onLine` spy. It is fine, but `mockRestore()` inside that one test would say so more clearly.
4. Concerns 2 and 3 in the report (refused learning items piling up as `needs_look`; a background refusal leaving a "Mission done" screen the server doesn't agree with) are already handled by ruling R18. Recording them here only; I spent no effort on them.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved

**Reasoning:** Every brief requirement and controller amendment is in place, and all seven named risks check out. Order, deletions, the finish-screen fallback, userId from the server and the double-tap guard are all sound, and the `learning.spec` wait is legitimate synchronization. The one real defect is the "Saved on this device." note flashing during every online save, a UX polish fix worth folding into the R18 fix round.

### Findings

1. **R18, refused learning items deleted: ADDRESSED.**
   - `src/features/offline/outbox/state.ts:~17-21` returns `null` for `refused` when `item.kind !== "purchase"`. It logs `logger.warn("Refused outbox item dropped", { kind })`, with no payload and no message. Purchases still go to `needs_look`.
   - `state.test.ts` has both required tests, `a refused learning item is deleted` (loops all four learning kinds) and `a refused purchase becomes needs_look`.
   - `send.test.ts` was adjusted to use `kind: "purchase"` so the existing assertion still holds.
   - On check (a): the log text is acceptable. It carries only `kind`, and `send.ts` already logs each refusal separately.

2. **Pending indicators only after a failed send or while offline: ADDRESSED.**
   - The shared rule is `showsAsPending(item, online)` in `state.ts` (end of file): `needs_look || attempts > 0 || !online`. It is exported from the `src/features/offline/index.ts` barrel.
   - `SavedOnDevice` in `mission-player.tsx` (~:468) uses it and excludes `needs_look`, so no learning needs-look UI appears.
   - `pending-purchases.tsx` filters with the same rule. The refresh counter still uses the unfiltered `stored.length`, so the `router.refresh()` on an item leaving is unaffected. Good catch by the implementer.
   - The required tests exist: `MissionPlayer > shows no saved-on-device note while an online save is in flight` (also covers the offline case), `PendingPurchases > shows no card while an online save is in flight`, and a `showsAsPending` unit test.

3. **Type guards for `saved.data`: ADDRESSED.**
   - `isSummary` and `isCheckInResult` in `mission-player.tsx` (~:617-631) use `Number.isInteger` checks. The call sites fall back to local state (`attemptSummary(allResults)`, or a local correct count) on a mismatch.
   - A real unexpected-shape test exists for completeMission.
   - The check-in "unexpected shape" test passes `data: null`, which is the queued path, not a wrong shape. It is weak but harmless (Minor).
   - On check (d): the fallback is silent, with no log. The fallback is local grading of the same answers, so the child sees correct data and a server shape drift would not be user-visible. Observability is the only cost. Minor: add a `logger.warn` with no data if you want drift detected.

### New Breakage in Fix Diff

No Critical or Important breakage. Two points you asked about:

- **(b) R19 gaps: Minor.** Checked `flush.ts` (BUSY and `drain`) and `use-online.ts`.
  - A network failure calls `reportNetworkFailure()`, which sets `online=false`, so the note shows even at attempts 0. A non-network failure (5xx, throw) goes to `retry`, which makes attempts > 0 and the note shows. Both cases are covered.
  - **BUSY in another tab (lock held):** the holder drains the whole user queue and either sends the items or increments attempts, so the note appears if sending fails. Items sitting at attempts 0 while online are in flight in the other tab. The only gap is if that tab dies mid-flush. Then the items sit with no note until the next trigger (OutboxSync mount, online event, or the next submit) sends them. That window is short and the next page load closes it.
  - **Before OutboxSync's first flush:** the items sit with no note until the flush starts. This is brief and the same as above.
  - **Unauthorized (signed out):** `nextState` leaves the item unchanged at attempts 0 while online, so there is no indicator. That is arguably right, because the user is signed out. The data is still safe on the device.
  - **After reconnecting:** the note hides immediately and returns only if a send fails, as you predicted. That matches R19. Severity is Minor.
- **(c) e2e change: sound.**
  - It still asserts the exact order `[step x6, completeMission]` via `expect.poll(() => sent)`. It adds a poll for 7 `requestfinished` events, which waits on real responses.
  - The server state is still asserted through "Back to Learn", `aria-current="step"` on Day 2, and "Practised".
  - `toHaveCount(0)` on the note right after going back online is valid because nothing had been sent, so attempts are 0 and the note hides on the `online` event.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- Learning items that go to `needs_look` through the `retry` path (20 attempts or older than 7 days, `state.ts` `tooOld` branch) are still parked and hidden. They leak the same way R18 was meant to stop. R18 only covers `refused`, but a ruling on the expired-learning case may be worth making.
- The mission-player test `afterEach` uses `vi.restoreAllMocks()` for only one spy. This is a style nit.

### Verdict
All findings addressed: yes

No Critical or Important breakage. Two points you asked about:

- **(b) R19 gaps: Minor.** Checked `flush.ts` (BUSY and `drain`) and `use-online.ts`.
  - A network failure calls `reportNetworkFailure()`, which sets `online=false`, so the note shows even at attempts 0. A non-network failure (5xx, throw) goes to `retry`, which makes attempts > 0 and the note shows. Both cases are covered.
  - **BUSY in another tab (lock held):** the holder drains the whole user queue and either sends the items or increments attempts, so the note appears if sending fails. Items sitting at attempts 0 while online are in flight in the other tab. The only gap is if that tab dies mid-flush. Then the items sit with no note until the next trigger (OutboxSync mount, online event, or the next submit) sends them. That window is short and the next page load closes it.
  - **Before OutboxSync's first flush:** the items sit with no note until the flush starts. This is brief and the same as above.
  - **Unauthorized (signed out):** `nextState` leaves the item unchanged at attempts 0 while online, so there is no indicator. That is arguably right, because the user is signed out. The data is still safe on the device.
  - **After reconnecting:** the note hides immediately and returns only if a send fails, as you predicted. That matches R19. Severity is Minor.
- **(c) e2e change: sound.**
  - It still asserts the exact order `[step x6, completeMission]` via `expect.poll(() => sent)`. It adds a poll for 7 `requestfinished` events, which waits on real responses.
  - The server state is still asserted through "Back to Learn", `aria-current="step"` on Day 2, and "Practised".
  - `toHaveCount(0)` on the note right after going back online is valid because nothing had been sent, so attempts are 0 and the note hides on the `online` event.

### Out-of-Scope Observations

- Learning items that go to `needs_look` through the `retry` path (20 attempts or older than 7 days, `state.ts` `tooOld` branch) are still parked and hidden. They leak the same way R18 was meant to stop. R18 only covers `refused`, but a ruling on the expired-learning case may be worth making.
- The mission-player test `afterEach` uses `vi.restoreAllMocks()` for only one spy. This is a style nit.

### Verdict
All findings addressed: yes

## Task Description

Binding project-wide constraints: /home/ezio/Desktop/Work/8x/Jarso/.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-30-network-resilience/global-constraints.md (offline copy "You're offline, try again when connected"; calm styling; no blaming words).

## Context
Part of a network-resilience plan for Jarso (Next.js 16 + Supabase pocket-money app). Parent and account actions must never send late: offline they show the warning and keep what was typed; on a slow network they stop after 15 s with the slow copy. Next's `experimental.useOffline` is deliberately NOT enabled, so a plain Server Action call either reaches the server or rejects.

Built already in `@/features/offline` (client barrel): `runOnline<T>(action: () => Promise<ActionResult<T>>, { timeoutMs? })` → resolves `ActionResult<T>`; refuses without calling the action only when `navigator.onLine === false`; maps a rejection to `{ ok:false, error:{ code:"UNAVAILABLE", message: offlineCopy.offline } }`, a 15 s timeout to `offlineCopy.slow`, a "Failed to find Server Action" rejection to `offlineCopy.updated`. `useOnlineActionState<T>(action: (prev: ActionState<T>, payload: FormData) => Promise<ActionResult<T>>)` returns the same tuple as `useActionState(action, idle)`.

Exact call sites to convert (controller grep of `Action(` / `useActionState` / `startTransition(` in src/features/*/components, excluding tests): allowance: allowance-form, allowance-overview, allowance-summary, gift-form, handover-form, pause-sheet; auth: sign-in-form, sign-up-form, sign-out-button; diary: undo-button; family: accept-invitation-form, add-child-form, child-sign-in-form, create-family-form, family-settings-form, refresh-invitations-button (router.refresh — leave it as is, it isn't an action), remove-child-dialog, rename-child-form, set-pin-form, start-onboarding-form; guide: guide-sheet, guide-switch; profiles: display-name-form, parent-details-form, and the profile completion dialog if it calls an action; rules: overspend-switch, rule-row. NOT these (already moved to the outbox): diary/purchase-sheet, learning/mission-player (except its startMissionAction, already wrapped). Re-run the grep yourself to catch anything missed (also `src/components`).

Rules:
- `useActionState(fn, idle)` → `useOnlineActionState(fn)`; direct/`startTransition` calls → wrap the action call in `runOnline(() => action(...))`. Existing `FormError`/toast/inline paths show `result.error.message`. Keep what was typed.
- Some actions `redirect()` on success (e.g. sign-in, sign-out, create family): a redirect from a Server Action surfaces as a navigation, not an ActionResult — make sure wrapping doesn't swallow it (check how runOnline handles a thrown NEXT_REDIRECT in the client; Server Action redirects in Next 16 are applied by the router, the promise may never resolve or may reject — read node_modules/next/dist/docs on Server Actions + redirect and test sign-out e2e). If runOnline would turn a redirect into the offline/slow copy, fix it in runOnline (e.g. don't time out once the response has started, or rethrow Next control-flow errors via `unstable_rethrow`-equivalent on the client) and add a unit test; report what you found.
- Where a component renders an error message, offline copy must use calm styling, not destructive red, if that component distinguishes; if a component only has `FormError` (red), it's acceptable to keep it for now — note which ones in the report.
- One commit per feature area (allowance, auth, diary, family, guide, profiles, rules), e.g. `fix(allowance): warn instead of hanging when offline`. Conventional Commits, ≤60-char subject, NO AI attribution. `pnpm check` green after each feature (known flakes: rule-row.test.tsx, purchase-sheet.test.tsx under load — rerun once).
- e2e: `tests/e2e/offline-parent.spec.ts` with the brief's two tests; add a Playwright project in playwright.config.ts like the existing `offline-purchase` / `offline-mission` ones. Then run the full `pnpm test:e2e`.

### Spec Compliance
- **Verdict: meets the brief.** Every online-only Server Action call site in `src/features/*/components` and `src/components` is now wrapped. Nothing extra was added and nothing was misread.
- **Check (a), coverage.** I grepped for `Action(` and `useActionState(` and removed lines already wrapped in `runOnline` or `useOnlineActionState`.
  - The only unwrapped calls left are `src/features/diary/components/purchase-sheet.tsx:76` and `src/features/learning/components/mission-player.tsx:186`. Both are excluded because they use the outbox.
  - `allowance-overview` RowAction and `CardAction` are name matches, not action calls.
  - I listed every component that imports `server/actions`: all 25 are converted or excluded.
  - No client component in `src/app` imports an action.
  - **No misses.**
- **Check (b), redirects.**
  - `unstable_rethrow` comes from `next/navigation`, which is correct. Next ships a browser build (`next/dist/client/components/unstable-rethrow.browser.js`) that rethrows when `isNextRouterError` is true.
  - I confirmed in `server-action-reducer.js:236-254` that an action redirect *rejects* the action promise with a redirect error while the router navigates. So the rethrow is needed, and the new unit test matches what really happens.
  - Redirect and notFound now pass through untouched. This is the same as the bare `useActionState(signIn, idle)` code had before Task 5.
  - **Slow redirect (sign-in that redirects after 16 s):** at 15 s the user sees "This is taking a while. Try again?" and the button is enabled again. When the response finally arrives, the reducer still navigates, so the user is moved on without warning.
  - There is no unhandled rejection: `Promise.race` already attached a handler, so the late redirect rejection is absorbed.
  - The only risk is a retry inside that window on a redirecting create action (add-child, create-family, accept-invitation). That is a side effect of Task 5's timeout design, not of this task. Listed as Minor below.
- **Check (c), calm styling.**
  - `FormError calm` (`src/components/form/field.tsx:98-106`) uses `text-ink-2`, `role="status"` and no icon. The red path is unchanged.
  - Every converted site passes `calm` only when `code === "UNAVAILABLE"`. Validation and server errors still render red, and their copy is unchanged.
  - Two intentional copy changes, both consistent with the global constraints: guide-sheet's local "We couldn't reach the guide…" and guide-switch's catch copy were replaced by the global offline copy.
- **Check (d), guide 45 s.** This matches the constraint ("Whole guide turn: 45 000 ms"), but it ties exactly with `TURN_DEADLINE_MS = 45_000` at `src/features/guide/server/pipeline.ts:98`. See Minor 1.
- **Check (e), e2e.**
  - `tests/e2e/offline-parent.spec.ts` covers both scenarios from the brief: offline confirm warns, after reconnect and 5 s the item is still due, confirming again clears Due now; sign-out offline warns and the user stays signed in after reload.
  - Assertions are scoped to the `form` element, not the banner.
  - "Confirmed once" is shown only indirectly, by `dueNow()` having count 0. That is acceptable because the offline click returns early on `navigator.onLine === false` and never sends a request.
  - Note: this means the e2e never exercises the fetch-rejection `catch` path. The unit tests do.
- **Check (f), flakiness.** ⚠️ Not verifiable. All three tests reported as flaky touch converted code: the profile sign-out dialog uses SignOutButton, settings remove uses `remove-child-dialog`, and onboarding parent-started uses accept-invitation and create-family.
  - However, the Playwright config sets no expect timeout, so the default 5 s applies. The 15 s client timeout can therefore only fire after an expect has already failed, so the timeout cannot cause a new failure.
  - On the online happy path the only change is the wrapper plus `reportNetworkSuccess`.
  - The baseline also failed the sign-out dialog test.
  - I found no mechanism for a regression, so I judge these to be load flakes. There is no baseline evidence for settings remove or onboarding.

### Strengths
- The redirect fix has the right root cause, and its test was shown failing first (RED). Online sign-out in the e2e reaching `/login` proves it end to end.
- Wrapping only the action inside `useActionState` callbacks keeps a late success after a timeout from firing a toast or navigation. Idempotency keys (handover, gift, guide `clientMessageId`) make a retry safe.
- guide-sheet loses its duplicate offline handling, and guide-switch's try/catch goes away.
- Sign-out now shows an error at all; before, it showed nothing.
- The e2e correctly anticipates that the banner repeats the offline copy and scopes its assertions to the form.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
None beyond the known gap the controller already owns: uncontrolled fields are cleared after a refused submit.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **Guide timeout tie:** `src/features/guide/components/guide-sheet.tsx:46` uses `TURN_TIMEOUT_MS = 45_000`, the same as the server pipeline deadline. The server's 45 s starts after auth and context loading, and the response still has to travel back.
   - So the client nearly always gives up first. The child sees the slow copy instead of the server's `fallbackResult` reply (`pipeline.ts:142`).
   - A retry does recover the saved fallback, because the id is reused.
   - Fix: about 52 s on the client, still under `maxDuration = 60`. The plan states 45 s, so this needs a call from the controller.
2. **Repeated calm logic:** `calm={state?.ok === false && state.error.code === "UNAVAILABLE"}` is repeated at roughly 18 sites. On top of that, three different patterns hold calm state: a separate `calm` useState (allowance-summary, gift, handover, overspend, rule-row), a `{text, calm}` object (undo-button, guide-sheet, guide-switch), and derived from state.
   - Fix: let `FormError` accept the error object (`error?: AppErrorShape`) and work out the message and calm itself.
3. **Hand-rolled styling:** `undo-button.tsx:825-828`, `guide-sheet.tsx:1561-1563` and `guide-switch.tsx:1671-1673` repeat the calm/alert styling by hand instead of reusing `FormError`.
4. **Sign-out layout:** `sign-out-button.tsx:710` adds `grid justify-items-end gap-1` to the form. This may shift the button in containers that relied on the old inline form (header, profile-completion dialog). ⚠️ Not checked visually.
5. **Late redirect after timeout:** on redirecting actions (add-child, create-family, accept-invitation, sign-in), the slow copy shows at 15 s and the late redirect then navigates anyway. A retry in that window can send a duplicate create. This comes from Task 5's timeout design, so only worth noting.
6. **Missing docs entry:** `docs/testing.md` has no entry for the new `offline-parent` Playwright project.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Approved
**Reasoning:** Every call site is converted, redirects pass through correctly (checked against the Next 16 reducer), and calm styling is applied only to `UNAVAILABLE`, with real errors still red and their copy unchanged. The remaining items are a timeout tuning choice, small DRY cleanups, and the form-reset gap the controller already owns.

   - Converted to `onSubmit={submit}`: sign-in, sign-up, add-child, child-sign-in, create-family, start-onboarding and accept-invitation (via the hook), plus family-settings, remove-child, rename-child and set-pin (via `useFormSubmit(action[, state])`).
   - Unit test `useOnlineActionState > submit keeps uncontrolled field values after a refused submit` is at `online-action.test.tsx:~1890`. It goes offline, submits, and asserts that the action was not called and that "Sam" is still in the input.
   - The e2e is `tests/e2e/offline-parent.spec.ts:~2187` (rename). It fills "Sammy", goes offline, saves, expects the offline copy in the dialog and `toHaveValue("Sammy")`, then goes online and saves successfully.
   - I could not see these forms' field state in the diff, so I did not check that the forms still on `action=` (allowance-form, handover-form, parent-details-form, gift-form, pause-sheet, display-name-form) have no uncontrolled fields. gift note, pause message and display name are controlled, which the diff shows. The other three are not in the 10 forms listed in the ruling, so I assume they are controlled.

2. **R21, guide timeout 52 s: ADDRESSED.** `src/features/guide/components/guide-sheet.tsx:~1661` sets `TURN_TIMEOUT_MS = 52_000` with a comment: the server deadline is 45 s, the extra time lets its reply arrive first, and it stays under `maxDuration` 60 s.

3. **R22, FormError owns calm vs red: ADDRESSED.**
   - `src/components/form/field.tsx:~485-512`: `FormError({ error, message })` picks calm when `error.code === "UNAVAILABLE"` and red otherwise.
   - Every `calm={state?.ok === false && ...}` expression is gone.
   - The `calm` `useState` is gone from allowance-summary, gift-form, handover-form, overspend-switch and rule-row, and the `{text, calm}` state is gone from undo-button, guide-sheet and guide-switch.

4. **Minor, docs/testing.md: ADDRESSED.** A row for `offline-parent` is added at `docs/testing.md:~453`, and its description matches the spec, including the rename case. `offline-mission` and `offline-purchase` rows already existed.

### Specific checks

- **(a) PIN and password handling.**
  - The `useFormSubmit` effect (`online-action.ts:~1976-1982`) empties every `input[type=password]` whenever `state` changes to anything except an `UNAVAILABLE` error.
  - So after a server refusal (VALIDATION, wrong PIN, lockout) or a success, the password and PIN are cleared. Only offline or slow results keep them, because the server never answered.
  - The unit test covers clearing after a refusal. Its title also says "not when offline", but no assertion covers that half, so that is a Minor test gap.
  - The initial `null` state returns early.
  - The effect only sets `value=""` on uncontrolled inputs. That works here because the PIN fields are uncontrolled.
  - The effect is wired only into `useOnlineActionState` and set-pin (`useFormSubmit(action, state)`). The only other form with a password-type field is sign-in/sign-up, which use `useOnlineActionState`. The other `useFormSubmit` forms (rename, family-settings, remove-child) have no password fields.
- **(b) onSubmit forms.**
  - Enter still works, because implicit submit fires `submit`.
  - `noValidate` is untouched on every form I could see.
  - `new FormData(form, submitter)` includes named hidden inputs: `next`, `childId`, `invite`, `invitationId`, `timezone`, and the Base UI `currency` and `timezone` Select inputs. It also includes the submitter's name and value.
  - `pending` still comes from `useActionState`. The `startTransition` call keeps `isPending` accurate, and the buttons keep `loading` or `disabled={pending}`.
  - The idempotency-key forms (handover, gift, guide) are not switched, so their keys are unaffected.
  - Side effect: typed values now also survive server VALIDATION refusals, which is an improvement.
- **(c) FormError refactor.**
  - Red errors keep `role="alert"` and the icon. Calm errors keep `role="status"` with `text-ink-2 text-sm`.
  - The guide error loses `font-semibold` and drops from base size to `text-sm`, and the red guide and undo errors now gain the alert icon. This is trivial, not a design regression, because a shared component is the intent of R22. Optionally check once that the guide error is readable for 11-14 year olds.
  - undo-button's wrapper changed from `span` to `div`, which is harmless.
- **(d) sign-out.** It still uses `action=`, which is correct because it has no fields. Its offline message stays visible and still goes through `FormError`.
- **(e) `.claude-logs` commit.** The visible hunks contain only prompts, reports, paths and a `users.noreply.github.com` email. I saw no credential-shaped strings.

None Critical or Important.

- Minor: the unit test title promises "not when offline", but no assertion covers it.
- Minor: after a slow timeout the PIN field keeps its value, although the request may have reached the server. A retry is possible, so this is fine.

### Spec Compliance
- `SlowFallback` (`src/components/shell/slow-fallback.tsx:1-37`): MET. Client component. Renders children, then after `after = 10_000` adds `offlineCopy.slow` ("This is taking a while. Try again?", checked in `src/features/offline/copy.ts`) and a "Try again" button calling `router.refresh()`. Calm styling (`text-ink-2 text-sm`, outline button).
- Barrel export `src/components/shell/index.ts:+1`: MET.
- Three required tests (`slow-fallback.test.tsx`): MET. Names differ slightly from the brief ("SlowFallback > ..."), but the behaviours match. The 9_999 / 10_000 boundary is tested and the unmount test asserts `getTimerCount` goes 1 to 0.
- (b) Timer: MET. `useEffect` returns `clearTimeout`. The fallback unmounts when the boundary resolves, so no timer outlives the fallback. Tested.
- (c) Page wiring: MET. All 12 listed pages wrap only the outermost page-level Suspense fallback.
  - The inner boundaries are untouched. On `parent/children/[childId]` the diff shows 58-67 as still plain skeletons, and only the line ~133 outer fallback changed.
  - Only the fallback prop changed; no Suspense moved or removed. Cache Components prerendering is unaffected, because the fallback is static client markup with no dynamic reads.
  - No page lost its skeleton.
- (d) Import of `@/features/offline` from `src/components/shell`: allowed.
  - `docs/conventions.md:42,55` allow barrel imports across modules, and the table marks `@/features/<f>` as client-safe.
  - The offline barrel comment says "Nothing here is server-only".
  - `grep` found no `components/shell` import inside `src/features/offline`, so there is no cycle.
  - `SlowFallback` is `"use client"`, so the barrel reaches only the client bundle. Server pages importing `@/components/shell` get a client reference for it, which is fine because `app-header` stays server-safe.
- (e) Accessibility: PARTIAL.
  - The slow block has `role="status"` and the button has a visible text label, "Try again".
  - The skeletons are `aria-hidden`, so a screen-reader user hears nothing until the slow block appears. See Minor 2 on announcement.
- (a) `router.refresh()` on a still-suspended boundary: UNCLEAR, not verified in a real browser.
  - Docs (`use-router.md:46`) say refresh makes a new server request and merges the RSC payload.
  - In the client router (`router-reducer/reducers/refresh-reducer.js`), refresh is modeled as a navigation to the current URL with `FreshnessPolicy.RefreshAll`. It invalidates the segment cache and BFCache and issues a new dynamic fetch, so it does not just wait on the stalled stream.
  - For a client-side navigation or an already-hydrated page this should help: the new request is independent of the stuck one. The `SlowFallback` instance should keep its `slow` state while the new request is pending.
  - For a hard load where the original HTML stream is itself hung, for example a dropped connection mid-stream, I could not establish that refresh recovers. The fresh fetch may succeed and swap the tree, but the evidence is thin.
  - The implementer admits the same: the report says "not verified in a real browser".
  - If the server is slow (not the connection), the new request will simply be slow again.
- Evidence gaps (the implementer flagged these):
  - The report lists the RED command and result only in prose (module not found), with no pasted output.
  - The GREEN output is also prose only ("3 passed").
  - There is no pasted `pnpm check` or `pnpm build` output (71 files / 656 tests is claimed), and no self-review section.
  - ⚠️ I did not re-run anything.

### Strengths
- The change is minimal and consistent. The same `<SlowFallback>` wrapper goes on every page, with no structural drift.
- It is a single client leaf with a default `after`, an `after` dependency in the effect, and a cleanup that clears the timer.
- The copy comes from the shared `offlineCopy.slow`, so the wording lives in one place.
- The tests use fake timers, cover both sides of the 10 s boundary and the unmount cleanup, and click through to `refresh`.
- The parent child page keeps the skeleton inside `PageShell`, so the layout is stable.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. Retry may not recover the exact case the feature targets (`slow-fallback.tsx:956`). The original bug is a skeleton stuck for 45 s.
   - `router.refresh()` is unverified against a stalled initial-load stream.
   - Pick one:
     - Run one manual or Playwright check that delays or hangs the RSC response, then clicks "Try again" and sees content.
     - Or make the button more robust: call `router.refresh()` first and fall back to `window.location.reload()` if the fallback is still showing after a short wait (for example 3-5 s).
   - If you do neither, the "Try again" button may be a no-op for a hung hard load.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. No feedback after clicking "Try again". The button stays enabled and shows no pending state, so a child may click repeatedly and queue refreshes. Consider a brief disabled or "Trying..." state.
2. `role="status"` is mounted together with its content. Some screen readers do not announce live regions inserted already populated. Render an empty `role="status"` container from the start and fill it when slow, or add `aria-live="polite"`.
3. Importing the whole `@/features/offline` barrel (outbox, online-action, and more) into a shell component pulls a lot of client code for one string. It is only in the client bundle and likely already loaded by the app shell, so this is not blocking. Check the bundle if size matters.
4. Test titles are not prefixed `SlowFallback >` as in the brief. The `describe` block supplies the prefix, so this is cosmetic.
5. The `.claude-logs` diff only changed the session log, as expected.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** Spec, structure, timer cleanup, barrel use and page wiring are all correct and clean. The one real doubt is whether `router.refresh()` unblocks a hung initial stream. It is unverified and the implementer's own fallback (`window.location.reload()`) was not applied, so one cheap verification or a reload fallback is needed.

- (a) auth-js verified. `GoTrueClient.js` `_signOut` lines 3443-3448: a non-ignored revoke error (`AuthRetryableFetchError` is not a 401/403/404 `AuthApiError`) hits line 3446; for scope other than 'others' it calls `removeCurrentSession()` at line 3447, then returns the error at 3449. The default scope is 'global', so the local session is removed. `_removeSession` (4421-4439) calls `removeItemAsync` on `this.storage` and `storageKey + '-user'`. In the SSR client `this.storage` is the cookie adapter, so removal goes through `setAll`. Server Actions can write cookies, so cookies are cleared. The try/catch around `setAll` in `src/lib/supabase/server.ts` only matters in Server Components.
- (b) confirmed: `AuthRetryableFetchError` is constructed as `super(message, 'AuthRetryableFetchError', status, undefined)`, so `.code` is undefined.
- (c) `redirect` sits after the `if`, outside any try in the action body. Tests expect a Redirect rejection in both the error and success cases, so redirect-on-failure is covered.
- (d) docs contain no credentials, project refs or keys. The log payload is code only: no token, no email.

### Strengths
- Minimal, focused diff. The auth-js claim is correct, and the code comment states why the redirect is safe.
- Tests cover the error path and the success path, including that no warning is logged on success.
- The docs paragraph records the reason for the proxy decision, and the runbook text is verbatim.

### Issues
#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)
1. The log is content-free for the main failure (actions.ts:438). For a timeout, `error.code` is undefined, so the warning says only "Sign-out revoke failed" with `code: undefined`. The test at actions.test.ts:406-408 asserts exactly that. The warning cannot tell a timeout from a 5xx. Log `{ name: error.name, status: error.status, code: error.code }`. All three are non-sensitive (a class name, an HTTP status and an error-code string). Update the test to expect `name: "AuthRetryableFetchError"`. The brief's `{ code }` format is plan-mandated, so this is a plan defect rather than an implementation slip.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. The test constructs `new AuthRetryableFetchError("x", 0)`. Status 0 is realistic for network failures, but the test never checks that the local session was cleared. That is auth-js behaviour, so it is acceptable.
2. The `.claude-logs` diff is noise, as expected.

### Assessment
**Task quality:** Needs fixes
**Reasoning:** Everything matches the brief and the auth-js claim is verified, including cookie clearing in a Server Action. The one real defect is that the log carries no information for `AuthRetryableFetchError`, which is the case this task targets. Logging `name` and `status` alongside `code` fixes it in a few lines. This is a small fix and the rest is fine.
